\{\upmu_{\perp}=\varepsilon^{2}\mu_{\perp}^{\varepsilon},\,\upmu_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}=\mu_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon},\,\uplambda=\varepsilon^{2}\lambda^{\varepsilon}\}
, where dimensionless viscosities
\{\mu_{\perp}^{\varepsilon},\,\mu_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon},\,\lambda^{\varepsilon}\}
satisfy (
10
), and resistivities
\{\upeta_{\perp}=\varepsilon^{2}\eta_{\perp}^{\varepsilon},\,\upeta_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}=\eta_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\}
, where dimensionless resistivities
\{\eta_{\perp}^{\varepsilon},\,\eta_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\}
satisfy (
11
). We then look for solution like

\displaystyle\left(\begin{tabular}[]{c}$\uprho/\bar{\uprho}$\\
$\text{v}/\bar{\text{v}}$\\
$\text{B}/\bar{\text{B}}$\end{tabular}\right)(\text{t},\text{x}_{\perp},\text{x}_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu})

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{tabular}[]{c}$\rho^{\varepsilon}\big(\text{t},{\varepsilon}^{-1}\,\text{x}_{\perp},\text{x}_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\big)$\\
$\varepsilon\,\,v^{\varepsilon}\big(\text{t},{\varepsilon}^{-1}\,\text{x}_{\perp},\text{x}_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\big)$\\
$1+\varepsilon\,B^{\varepsilon}\big(\text{t},{\varepsilon}^{-1}\,\text{x}_{\perp},\text{x}_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\big)$\end{tabular}\right)=\left(\begin{tabular}[]{c}$\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}}$\\
$0$\\
$1$\end{tabular}\right)+\varepsilon\left(\begin{tabular}[]{c}$\varrho^{\varepsilon}$\\
$v^{\varepsilon}$\\
$B^{\varepsilon}$\end{tabular}\right)\big(\text{t},{\varepsilon}^{-1}\,\text{x}_{\perp},\text{x}_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\big)

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{tabular}[]{c}$\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}}$\\
$0$\\
$1$\end{tabular}\right)+\varepsilon\left(\begin{tabular}[]{c}$\ \varrho^{\varepsilon}$\\
$v^{\varepsilon}$\\
$B^{\varepsilon}$\end{tabular}\right)\big(t,x_{\perp},x_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\big)\,,

where
\varrho^{\varepsilon}=(\rho^{\varepsilon}-\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}})/\varepsilon
. Plugging (
3.2
) into (
39
) leads to (
3
). Therefore, we investigate the dynamics of a magnetized plasma near a fixed large constant magnetic field where anisotropic oscillations in space can develop. The first term of the right-hand side of (
3.2
), which is of order of unity, is a stationary solution of (
39
). The second term of the right-hand side of (
3.2
) is the perturbation, which is of small amplitude (
\varepsilon\ll 1
) and of high frequency (
\varepsilon^{-1}\gg 1
). Such a framework belongs to the so called weakly nonlinear geometric optics regim.

4.
Asymptotic analysis in a periodic domain

This section is devoted to the proof of Theorem
1
. First, we obtain some weak compactness properties for the sequences,
\varrho^{\varepsilon}
,
B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}
,
v^{\varepsilon}
,
\rho^{\varepsilon}v^{\varepsilon}
, and
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
, and strong ones for the sequences
\rho^{\varepsilon}
,
B_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
,
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
,
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})
and
(\rho^{\varepsilon}v^{\varepsilon}-v^{\varepsilon})
. Using these compactness results, we justify the passage to the limit, in order, in the equations of
\rho^{\varepsilon}
,
\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
,
\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}
, and
B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}
(or equivalently
\varrho^{\varepsilon}
). For the equations of
\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
, we use the unitary group method, while for the equations of
\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}
, and
B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}
, we use some particular cancellations and a compactness argument (Lemma
14
of Appendix
A
).

4.1.
Compactness of
\rho^{\varepsilon}
and
\varrho^{\varepsilon}

Here, we aim at proving the following lemma.

Lemma 1
.

Assume
\gamma>3/2
. The sequences
{\rho^{\varepsilon}}
and
\varrho^{\varepsilon}:=(\rho^{\varepsilon}-\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}})/\varepsilon
satisfy the following properties.

\displaystyle\rho^{\varepsilon}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt}1\ \ \mbox{ \rm in }\ L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\gamma}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\!-\!\mbox{\rm strong}\,,

\displaystyle\varrho^{\varepsilon}\xrightharpoonup{\hskip 8.19447pt}\varrho\ \ \mbox{ \rm in }\ L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\kappa}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\!-\!\mbox{\rm weak--}\ast\,,\quad\kappa=\min\{2,\gamma\}\,.

Proof.
Let us start with
\rho^{\varepsilon}
. From energy inequality (
30
)-(
33
) with the pressure term
\Pi_{1}
, we already know that
\Pi_{1}(\rho^{\varepsilon})
is bounded in
L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
, uniformly with respect to
\varepsilon
. Thus, by weak compactness, we have
\rho^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow 1
in
L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\gamma}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\!-\!\mbox{\rm weak--}\ast
. In addition, since
\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}}=\overline{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}}\rightarrow 1
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, for
\varepsilon
small enough we have
\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\in(1/2,3/2)
. Then, using Lemma
11
, we claim that there exists
\eta=\eta_{\delta}=\eta(\gamma,\delta)>0
, such that for
|x-\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}}|\geq\delta
and
x\geq 0
, we have

x^{\gamma}-\gamma x\bar{x}^{\gamma-1}+(\gamma-1)\bar{x}^{\gamma}\geq\eta_{\delta}\big|x-\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\big|^{\gamma}.

(58)

Indeed, using Lemma
11
with
\bar{x}=\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\in(1/2,3/2)
, we obtain,
\eta=\nu_{3}
, for
1<\gamma<2
, and
3/2<R<x
;
\eta=\delta^{2-\gamma}\nu_{2}
, for
1<\gamma<2
, and
x\leq R
; and
\eta=\nu_{1}\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}|x-\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}}|^{2-\gamma}>0
, for
\gamma\geq 2
, and
x\geq 0
. Therefore, using (
58
), inequality
(a/2+b/2)^{\gamma}\leq(a^{\gamma}+b^{\gamma})/2
(by convexity of
x\mapsto x^{\gamma}
), and energy inequality (
30
)-(
33
) with the pressure term
\Pi_{2}
, we obtain

\sup_{t\geq 0}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1|^{\gamma}\leq 2^{\gamma-1}\big|{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\big|\big|\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}}-1\big|^{\gamma}+2^{\gamma-1}\sup_{t\geq 0}\bigg\{\int_{|\rho^{\varepsilon}-\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}}|\leq\delta}dx+\int_{|\rho^{\varepsilon}-\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}}|>\delta}dx\bigg\}\big|\rho^{\varepsilon}-\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\big|^{\gamma}\\
\leq 2^{\gamma-1}\bigg\{\big|{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\big|\big|\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}}-1\big|^{\gamma}+\big|{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\big|\delta^{\gamma}+\frac{C_{0}\varepsilon^{2}}{\eta_{\delta}}\bigg\}.

In the previous estimate, taking first
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, and then
\delta\rightarrow 0
, lead to the convergence of
\rho^{\varepsilon}
as stated in Lemma
1
. We continue with
\varrho^{\varepsilon}:=(\rho^{\varepsilon}-\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}})/\varepsilon
. Using Lemma
11
with
\bar{x}=\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\in(1/2,3/2)
and
x=\rho^{\varepsilon}
, and using energy inequality (
30
)-(
33
) with the pressure term
\Pi_{2}
, there exists a constant
C
independent of
\varepsilon
such that

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mbox{if }\ \gamma\geq 2,\ \ \|\varrho^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}\leq C\,,\\
&\mbox{if }\ \gamma<2,\ \forall R\in\big(\tfrac{3}{2},+\infty\big),\ \|\varrho^{\varepsilon}\mathbbm{1}_{\rho^{\varepsilon}<R}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}\leq C\,,\ \|\varrho^{\varepsilon}\mathbbm{1}_{\rho^{\varepsilon}\geq R}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\gamma}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}\leq C\varepsilon^{(2/\gamma)-1}\,.\end{aligned}\right.

(59)

Using this last estimate,
\varrho^{\varepsilon}
is bounded, uniformly with respect to
\varepsilon
, in
L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\kappa}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
, with
\kappa=\min\{2,\gamma\}
. Hence, weak compactness leads to the convergence of
\varrho^{\varepsilon}
stated in Lemma
1
.

\square

4.2.
Compactness of
B^{\varepsilon}

Here, we aim at proving the following lemma.

Lemma 2
.

The sequence
{B^{\varepsilon}}
satisfies the following properties.

\displaystyle B^{\varepsilon}\xrightharpoonup{\hskip 8.19447pt}B\ \ \mbox{ \rm in }\ L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{6}\cap H^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\!-\!\mbox{\rm weak}\cap L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\!-\!\mbox{\rm weak--}\ast\,,

\displaystyle\nabla_{\varepsilon}\cdot B^{\varepsilon}\xrightharpoonup{\hskip 8.19447pt}\nabla_{\perp}\cdot B_{\perp}=0\ \ \mbox{ \rm in }\ L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\!-\!\mbox{\rm weak}\,,

\displaystyle B_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt}B_{\perp}\ \ \mbox{ \rm in }\ L_{\rm loc}^{r}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\!-\!\mbox{\rm strong}\,,\quad 1\leq r<+\infty\,.

Proof.
The first limit of Lemma
2
, comes on the one hand from weak compactness, and on the other hand from energy inequality (
30
)-(
33
) with the pressure term
\Pi_{2}
, and the continuous Sobolev embeddings
H^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})\hookrightarrow L^{6}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
, which implies that
B^{\varepsilon}
is bounded in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{1}\cap L^{6}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
and in
L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
, uniformly with respect to
\varepsilon
.

We continue with the second assertion. Since
B^{\varepsilon}
is uniformly bounded in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
and
\nabla_{\perp}\cdot B_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}=-\varepsilon\partial_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
, we obtain
\|\nabla_{\perp}\cdot B_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}\leq\varepsilon\|\partial_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}\leq C_{0}\varepsilon
. On the one hand
\nabla_{\perp}\cdot B_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
is bounded in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
and goes to
\nabla_{\perp}\cdot B_{\perp}\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
. On the other hand, it must vanish as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Hence, the second line.

For the third assertion, we apply Lemma
13
of Appendix
A
with
\mathfrak{B}_{0}=H^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
,
\mathfrak{B}=L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
,
\mathfrak{B}_{1}=H^{-1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
,
p=r
, and
q=\infty
. To this end, we have to check the corresponding hypotheses. Below (and after when there is no possible ambiguity),
bounded
means “uniformly bounded with respect to
\varepsilon
”.

•

From energy inequality (
30
)-(
33
) and the continuous Sobolev embedding
H^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})\hookrightarrow L^{6}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
, we obtain that
B^{\varepsilon}
is bounded in
L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\cap L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{1}\cap L^{6}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
.

•

Obviously,
B_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
is bounded in
L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\cap L_{\rm loc}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
.

•

The final step is to estimate
\partial_{t}B_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
. We can exploit equation (
1
) to express this time derivative. Observe that, as can be seen at the level of (
23
), there is no singular term in
\varepsilon
. The Laplacian parts are clearly bounded in
L_{\rm loc}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
. Let us consider the products of components of
B^{\varepsilon}
and
v^{\varepsilon}
. We refer to (the proof of) Lemma
3
which guarantees that
v^{\varepsilon}
is bounded in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{1}\cap L^{6}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
. Hence,
by Hölder inequality, these products are bounded in
L_{\rm loc}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{3}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
. Since
L^{3}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})\hookrightarrow L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
, after spatial derivation, we find as required a bound in
L_{\rm loc}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
for
\partial_{t}B_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
.

\square

4.3.
Compactness of
v^{\varepsilon}
and
\rho^{\varepsilon}v^{\varepsilon}

Here we aim at proving the following lemma.

Lemma 3
.

Assume
\gamma>3/2
.
Let
\mathfrak{s}:=\max\{1/2,3/\gamma-1\}\in[1/2,1)
. The sequences
v^{\varepsilon}
and
\rho^{\varepsilon}v^{\varepsilon}
satisfy the following properties.

\displaystyle v^{\varepsilon}\xrightharpoonup{\hskip 8.19447pt}v\ \ \mbox{ \rm in }\ L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{6}\cap H^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\!-\!\mbox{\rm weak}\,,

\displaystyle\nabla_{\varepsilon}\cdot v^{\varepsilon}\xrightharpoonup{\hskip 8.19447pt}\nabla_{\perp}\cdot v_{\perp}=0\ \ \mbox{ \rm in }\ L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\!-\!\mbox{\rm weak}\,,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\xrightharpoonup{\hskip 8.19447pt}{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}\ \mbox{ {and} }\ {\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\xrightharpoonup{\hskip 8.19447pt}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}=0\ \ \mbox{ \rm in }\ L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{6}\cap H^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\!-\!\mbox{\rm weak}\,,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt}{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}=v_{\perp}\ \ \mbox{ \rm in }\ L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{p}\cap H^{s}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\!-\!\mbox{\rm strong}\,,\quad 1\leq p<6\,,\quad 0\leq s<1\,,

\displaystyle\rho^{\varepsilon}v^{\varepsilon}\xrightharpoonup{\hskip 8.19447pt}v\ \ \mbox{ \rm in }\ L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{q}\cap H^{-\upsigma}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\!-\!\mbox{\rm weak}\,,\quad\forall\,\upsigma\geq\mathfrak{s}:=\max\Bigl\{\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{\gamma-1}\Bigr\}\,,\quad q=\frac{6\gamma}{6+\gamma}\,,

\displaystyle\rho^{\varepsilon}v^{\varepsilon}-v^{\varepsilon}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt}0\ \ \mbox{ \rm in }\ L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{q}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\!-\!\mbox{\rm strong}\,,\quad q={6\gamma}/({6+\gamma})\,,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt}{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}=v_{\perp}\ \ \mbox{ \rm in }\ L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{q}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\!-\!\mbox{\rm strong}\,,\quad q={6\gamma}/({6+\gamma})\,.

Proof.
We start with the first statement of Lemma
3
. From energy inequality (
30
)-(
33
) with the pressure term
\Pi_{2}
, we obtain that
v^{\varepsilon}
is bounded in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};\dot{H}^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
. Let us show that
v^{\varepsilon}
is bounded in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
. From Poincaré–Wirtinger inequality and Hölder inequality, it is easy to show that

\|\cdot\|_{H^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})}^{2}\sim|\overline{(\cdot)}|^{2}+\|\cdot\|_{\dot{H}^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})}^{2}\,,\quad\mbox{ with }\quad\overline{(\cdot)}\equiv|{\mathbb{T}}^{3}|^{-1}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,\cdot\,.

There remains to control the mean value
\overline{v^{\varepsilon}}
. Using Hölder inequality, the embedding
L^{6}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})\hookrightarrow L^{2\gamma/(\gamma-1)}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
for
\gamma>3/2
, the continuous embedding
H_{0}^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})\hookrightarrow L^{6}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
(with
H_{0}^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
the set of zero-average functions in
\dot{H}^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
), we obtain for any
T>0
,

\begin{array}[]{rl}\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,\rho^{\varepsilon}\big|v^{\varepsilon}-\overline{v^{\varepsilon}}\big|^{2}&\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}dt\,\|\rho^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\gamma}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})}\big\|v^{\varepsilon}-\overline{v^{\varepsilon}}\big\|_{L^{2\gamma/(\gamma-1)}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})}^{2}\\
&\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}dt\,\|\rho^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\gamma}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})}\big\|v^{\varepsilon}-\overline{v^{\varepsilon}}\big\|_{L^{6}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})}^{2}\\
&\displaystyle\leq\|\rho^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\gamma}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}\|\nabla v^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}^{2}\leq{C}<\infty\,.\end{array}

(60)

Using inequality
b^{2}/2\leq(a-b)^{2}+a^{2}
and the mass conservation law, we obtain

\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,\rho^{\varepsilon}(\overline{v^{\varepsilon}})^{2}=\|\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})}(\overline{v^{\varepsilon}})^{2}\leq 2\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,\rho^{\varepsilon}(v^{\varepsilon}-\overline{v^{\varepsilon}})^{2}+2\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,\rho^{\varepsilon}(v^{\varepsilon})^{2}.

With (
60
), this implies (uniformly in
\varepsilon
)

(\overline{v^{\varepsilon}})^{2}\leq 2\,T^{-1}\,\|\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}^{-1}\big({C}+T\,\|\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}v^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}^{2}\big)\leq\widetilde{C}\,.

This information combined with the bound of
v^{\varepsilon}
in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};\dot{H}^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
indicates that
v^{\varepsilon}
is bounded in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
.

We continue with the second statement. To this end, we look at the mass conservation law (
20
). Since
\rho^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow 1
in
L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\gamma}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
–strong (Lemma
1
),
v^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup v
in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{6}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
–weak and
\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow 1
in
L^{\gamma}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
–strong (from (
27
) and by following the proof of Lemma
1
), it is easy to pass to the limit in the distributional sense in the linear terms. For the nonlinear term, we writte
\rho^{\varepsilon}\,v^{\varepsilon}=(\rho^{\varepsilon}-1)\,v^{\varepsilon}+v^{\varepsilon}
. Since
1/\gamma+1/6<1
(recall that
\gamma>3/2
), the first term vanishes strongly in
L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{6\gamma/(6+\gamma)}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
. At the limit, we recover for any test function
\varphi
that

\int_{\Omega}dx\,\varphi(0)+\int_{0}^{\infty}dt\int_{\Omega}dx\,\big(\partial_{t}\varphi+v_{\perp}\cdot\nabla_{\perp}\varphi\big)=\int_{0}^{\infty}dt\int_{\Omega}dx\,v_{\perp}\cdot\nabla_{\perp}\varphi=0\,,

which means that
\nabla_{\perp}\cdot v_{\perp}=0\,
in
\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
.

The third assertion of Lemma
3
is a consequence of the first and second statements of Lemma
3
, of the Helmhotz–Hodge decomposition
v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}={\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}+{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
and of the (weak) continuity properties of
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}
and
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}
.

The fourth assertion exploits some Gagliardo–Nirenberg interpolation inequalities together with delicate equicontinuity properties in time that require to already control the product
\rho^{\varepsilon}\,v^{\varepsilon}
. The proof is postponed to a later stage.

We pursue with the proof of fifth statement of Lemma
3
. On the one hand, from the uniform bounds
v^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{6}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
and
\rho^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\gamma}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
, and on the other hand, from Hölder inequality, we obtain
\rho^{\varepsilon}v^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{6\gamma/(6+\gamma)}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
uniformly with respect to
\varepsilon
, which gives, by weak compactness, the weak convergence of this sequence in this space. Moreover, from the first assertion of Lemma
1
and
3
, the product of
\rho^{\varepsilon}
and
v^{\varepsilon}
weakly converges to the limit point
v
in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{q}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
–weak with
1/q=1/\gamma+1/6
. The Sobolev embedding
H^{\mathfrak{s}}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})\hookrightarrow L^{q^{\prime}}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
, with
1/q^{\prime}=1-1/q=(5\gamma-6)/(6\gamma)
and
\mathfrak{s}\geq\max\{0,3/\gamma-1\}
, implies by duality that
L^{q}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})\hookrightarrow H^{-\mathfrak{s}}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
. Without loss of generality and in order to avoid further the multiplication of regularity indices we restrict
\mathfrak{s}
such that
\mathfrak{s}\geq\max\{1/2,3/\gamma-1\}
. We then have
\rho^{\varepsilon}v^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\mathfrak{s}}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
. We conclude by using the Sobolev embedding
H^{\upsigma}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})\hookrightarrow H^{\mathfrak{s}}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
for
\upsigma\geq\mathfrak{s}
, and duality.

From Hölder inequality, we have

\|\rho^{\varepsilon}v^{\varepsilon}-v^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{q}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}\leq\|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\gamma}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}\|v^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{6}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}\,,\qquad 1/q=1/\gamma+1/6\,.

Then, exploiting the first assertion of Lemmas
1
and
3
, we obtain the sixth statement of Lemma
3
.

We can now come back to the
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{p}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
–strong convergence in the fourth assertion of Lemma
3
. For this, we use Lemma
14
of Appendix
A
, for which we show below that its hypotheses, splitted in three points, are satisfied. 1) From the fifth statement of Lemma
3
, the selfadjointness of
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}
for the scalar product of
L^{2}(\Omega;{\mathbb{R}}^{2})
, and the continuity of
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}
in
L^{\alpha}(\Omega;{\mathbb{R}}^{2})
, for
1<\alpha<\infty
, we obtain
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})\rightharpoonup{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}=v_{\perp}
in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{6\gamma/(6+\gamma)}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
–weak. 2) The bound
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{6}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
and Lemma 4.3 in
[
7
]
implies
\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}(t,\cdot+h)-{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}(t,\cdot)\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{6}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}\rightarrow 0
, as
|h|\rightarrow 0
, uniformly with respect to
\varepsilon
. 3) Applying the Leray projector
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}
to equation (
21
) for
\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
, we obtain

\partial_{t}({\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}))=-\nabla_{\varepsilon}\cdot{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\otimes v^{\varepsilon})+\partial_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}B^{\varepsilon}-\nabla_{\varepsilon}\cdot{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}(B_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\otimes B^{\varepsilon})+\mu_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\Delta_{\perp}{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}+\mu_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\Delta_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\,.

(61)

Using the bounds
\rho^{\varepsilon}|v^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
,
v^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
, and
B^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
, and the following properties of the projector
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}
,
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}(H^{\alpha}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\hookrightarrow H^{\alpha}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
, with
\alpha\geq 0
, and
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}(L^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\hookrightarrow W^{-\delta,1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
, with
\delta>0
, we obtain from (
61
),
\partial_{t}({\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}))\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};(W^{-\delta-1,1}+H^{-1}+L^{2})({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\hookrightarrow L_{\rm loc}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};W^{-\delta-1,1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
. Gathering points 1) to 3), we can apply Lemma
14
of Appendix
A
with
g^{\varepsilon}=\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
,
h^{\varepsilon}={\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
,
p_{1}=q_{1}=2
(
1/p_{1}+1/q_{1}=1
),
p_{2}=6\gamma/(6+\gamma)
, and
q_{2}=6
(
1/p_{2}+1/q_{2}=1/\gamma+1/3<1
, for
\gamma>3/2
), to deduce that

{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})\cdot{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\xrightharpoonup{\hskip 8.19447pt}|{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}|^{2}=|v_{\perp}|^{2}\ \ \mbox{ \rm in }\ \ \mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})\,.

(62)

This limits leads to the strong convergence of
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
to
v_{\perp}
in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
. Indeed, using (
62
) and Hölder inequality, we obtain for any
T>0
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}^{2}-\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip-241.84842pt=\limsup_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\int_{0}^{T}dt\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,\big(|{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}-{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})\cdot{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\big)=\limsup_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\int_{0}^{T}dt\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\cdot v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}(1-\rho^{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle\hskip-243.27115pt\leq\limsup_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\gamma}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}\|v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2\theta}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}^{2}\,,

with
1/\gamma+1/\theta=1
. Since
\gamma>3/2
, we obtain
2\theta<6
. Therefore, using the first statement of Lemmas
1
and
3
, the right-hand side of the previous inequality vanishes, which leads to
\limsup_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}\leq\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}\geq\liminf_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}
and to
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}=v_{\perp}
in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
–strong, by Proposition 3.32 in
[
7
]
. This strong convergence in
L^{2}
allows to prove the fourth statement of Lemma
3
by using some interpolation inequalities. Indeed, using Gagliardo–Nirenberg inequality in space and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in time, we obtain

\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}-v_{\perp}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{p_{1}}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}\lesssim\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}-v_{\perp}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};\dot{H}^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}^{1/2}\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}-v_{\perp}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{p_{0}}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}^{1/2}\,,

(63)

with
1/p_{1}=1/12+1/(2p_{0})
and
\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}-v_{\perp}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};\dot{H}^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}<\infty
. Iterating inequality (
63
), we obtain a sequence of indices
p_{n}
such that
1/p_{n+1}=1/12+1/(2p_{n})
, with
p_{0}=2
, hence its limit
p_{\infty}=6
. This justifies the first part (strong convergence in
L^{p}
) of the fourth statement of Lemma
3
. For the second part of this statement, again, using Gagliardo–Nirenberg inequality in space (with not necessarily integers
[
8
,
9
]
) and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in time, we obtain

\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}-v_{\perp}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{s_{1}}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}\lesssim\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}-v_{\perp}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}^{1/2}\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}-v_{\perp}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{s_{0}}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}^{1/2}\,,

(64)

with
s_{1}=1/2+s_{0}/2
and
\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}-v_{\perp}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}<\infty
. Iterating inequality (
64
), we obtain a sequence of indices
s_{n}
such that
s_{n+1}=1/2+s_{n}/2
, with
s_{0}=0
, hence its limit
s_{\infty}=1
. This justifies the second part (strong convergence in
H^{s}
) of the fourth statement of Lemma
3
.

We finish with the proof the seventh statement. By triangular inequality and continuity of
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}
in
L^{\alpha}(\Omega;{\mathbb{R}}^{2})
, for
1<\alpha<\infty
, we obtain

\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}-v_{\perp})\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{q}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}\leq\|{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}-v_{\perp}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{q}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}+\|\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}-v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{q}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}.

Using the fourth and sixth assertions of Lemma
3
, and since
6/5<q<6
(because
1/q=1/\gamma+1/6
, and
3/2<\gamma<\infty
) this inequality allows to conclude.

\square

4.4.
Passage to the limit in the equation for
B_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}

Here, we justify the passage to the limit in (
23
) for
B_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
. Let us start with the initial condition term.
From the discussion about the properties of sequences of initial conditions in Section
2.3
(in particular the uniform bound (
27
) and the resulting convergences),
we can pass to the limit, in the distributional sense, in the initial condition term of (
23
) to obtain the limit initial condition
B_{0\perp}
.
Next, using on the one hand the third statement of Lemma
2
, and on the other hand the first and the second statements of Lemma
3
, we can pass to the limit, in the distributional sense, in all linear and nonlinear terms of equations (
23
) and (
25
) to obtain the first equation of (
12
)
and equation (
15
) in the sense of distributions.

4.5.
Passage to the limit in the equation for
\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}

Here, we justify the passage to the limit in equation (
21
) for
\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
, in several steps.
We start by recalling some basic tools. With
\Omega
being either
{\mathbb{T}}^{3}
or
{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
, we introduce the linear differential operator

\mathcal{L}:=-\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&c^{\varepsilon}\,\nabla_{\perp}\cdot\\
{}^{t}\nabla_{\perp}&0\end{array}\right),

(65)

defined on
\mathcal{D}_{0}^{\prime}(\Omega;{\mathbb{R}})\times\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega;{\mathbb{R}}^{2})
, where
\mathcal{D}_{0}^{\prime}(\Omega;{\mathbb{R}})=\{\phi\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega;{\mathbb{R}})\ |\ \int_{\Omega}dx\,\phi=0\}
, and such that
\mathcal{L}U=-\,{}^{t}(c^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{\perp}\cdot\Phi,\,{}^{t}\nabla_{\perp}\phi)
, with
U\equiv{}^{t}(\phi,{}^{t}\Phi)\in\mathcal{D}_{0}^{\prime}(\Omega;{\mathbb{R}})\times\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\Omega;{\mathbb{R}}^{2})
. Here
c^{\varepsilon}:=b^{\varepsilon}+1/\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}}
, with
b^{\varepsilon}:=b(\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}})^{\gamma-1}
. Since for
\varepsilon
small enough
\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\in(1/2,3/2)
, there exist constants
0<\underline{c}\leq\bar{c}<\infty
, such that
\underline{c}\leq c^{\varepsilon}\leq\bar{c}
, and
c^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow c=1+b=1+a\gamma
.

We claim that
\mathcal{L}
generates a one-parameter group of isometry
\{\mathcal{S}(\tau):=\exp(\tau\mathcal{L});\,\tau\in{\mathbb{R}}\}
, from
H^{\alpha}(\Omega;{\mathbb{R}})\times H^{\alpha}(\Omega;{\mathbb{R}}^{2})
into itself with the norm
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|U\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{H^{\alpha}(\Omega)}^{2}:=\|\phi\|_{H^{\alpha}(\Omega)}^{2}+c^{\varepsilon}\|\Phi\|_{H^{\alpha}(\Omega)}^{2}
, for all
\alpha\in{\mathbb{R}}
. Indeed, this comes from the fact that the operator
\mathcal{L}
is skew-adjoint for the scalar product
\left\langle\hskip-2.5pt\left\langle\cdot\,,\,\cdot\right\rangle\hskip-2.5pt\right\rangle
of
L^{2}(\Omega;{\mathbb{R}})\times L^{2}(\Omega;{\mathbb{R}}^{2})
, defined by
\left\langle\hskip-2.5pt\left\langle U,\,V\right\rangle\hskip-2.5pt\right\rangle=\langle\phi,\psi\rangle_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+c^{\varepsilon}\langle\Phi,\Psi\rangle_{L^{2}(\Omega)}
, where
U\equiv{}^{t}(\phi,{}^{t}\Phi)
,
V\equiv{}^{t}(\psi,{}^{t}\Psi)
, and the notation
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{L^{2}(\Omega)}
stands for the standard scalar product in
L^{2}(\Omega)
for scalar or vector valued functions. This isometry group can also be directly verified from the
H^{\alpha}
-energy estimates of the solutions
U(\tau)=\mathcal{S}(\tau)\,U_{0}
, satisfying the equation
\partial_{\tau}\,U(\tau)=\mathcal{L}\,U(\tau)
, i.e.,

\partial_{\tau}\phi+c^{\varepsilon}\,\nabla_{\perp}\cdot\Phi=0\,,\quad\partial_{\tau}\Phi+\nabla_{\perp}\phi=0\,.

(66)

Indeed, we first set
\Lambda=({\rm I}-\Delta)^{1/2}
and recall that
H^{\alpha}(\Omega)=\Lambda^{-\alpha}L^{2}(\Omega)
. Applying
\Lambda^{\alpha}
to equations (
66
), and taking the
L^{2}(\Omega)
scalar product of the result with
\Lambda^{\alpha}\phi
(resp.
c^{\varepsilon}\Lambda^{\alpha}\Phi
) for the first (resp. second) equation of (
66
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|U\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{H^{\alpha}(\Omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\left\langle\hskip-2.5pt\left\langle\Lambda^{\alpha}U,\Lambda^{\alpha}U\right\rangle\hskip-2.5pt\right\rangle=\frac{d}{dt}\big(\|\Lambda^{\alpha}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+c^{\varepsilon}\|\Lambda^{\alpha}\Phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}dx\,c^{\varepsilon}\big(\Lambda^{\alpha}\phi\nabla_{\perp}\cdot\Lambda^{\alpha}\Phi+\Lambda^{\alpha}\Phi\cdot\nabla_{\perp}\Lambda^{\alpha}\phi\big)=0\,.

In order to understand some properties of the group
\mathcal{S}(\tau)
, we denote by
\mathcal{S}_{1}(\tau)\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
\mathcal{S}_{2}(\tau)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
the components of
\mathcal{S}(\tau)
. Observe that
\int_{\Omega}dx\,\mathcal{S}_{1}(\tau)\,U
and
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}\mathcal{S}_{2}(\tau)\,U
are independent of
\tau\in{\mathbb{R}}
. In particular
\mathcal{S}(\tau)\,U
is independent of
\tau
, if
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}\Phi=0
(i.e.,
\nabla_{\perp}\cdot\Phi=0
) and if
\phi
is constant. From (
66
) the operator
\mathcal{L}
is equivalent to the transverse wave operator. Indeed, the equation
\partial_{\tau}\,U=\mathcal{L}\,U
is equivalent to the scalar wave equations
(\partial_{\tau}^{2}-c^{\varepsilon}\Delta_{\perp})\phi=0
, and
(\partial_{\tau}^{2}-c^{\varepsilon}\Delta_{\perp})\varphi=0
, where we have used the Helmholtz–Hodge decomposition
\Phi={\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}\Phi+\nabla_{\perp}\varphi
, with
\int_{\Omega}dx\,\varphi=0
,
\forall\,\tau\in{\mathbb{R}}
, and observing that
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}\Phi
is a constant determined by the initial conditions (since
\partial_{\tau}{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}\Phi=0
from the second equation of (
66
)).

The key to justify the limit of the equation for
\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
,
is to construct an approximate solution to the MHD equations (
20
)-(
25
), which allows
us to pass to the limit in both singular terms and nonlinear terms. Such a construction is given by the following lemma.

Lemma 4
.

Let us define
U^{\varepsilon}:={}^{t}(\phi^{\varepsilon},{}^{t}\Phi^{\varepsilon})
, where
\phi^{\varepsilon}:=b^{\varepsilon}\varrho^{\varepsilon}+B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon},\,
and
\ \Phi^{\varepsilon}:={\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}),\,
with
\ \varrho^{\varepsilon}:=(\rho^{\varepsilon}-\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}})/\varepsilon,\,
and
\ b^{\varepsilon}:=b(\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}})^{\gamma-1}
. Let
\mathfrak{s}
be the same positive real number as in Lemma
3
, i.e.,
\mathfrak{s}:=\max\{1/2,3/\gamma-1\}\in[1/2,1)
. Then,

1.

There exist functions
\,\mathcal{U}={}^{t}(\psi,{}^{t}\Psi)\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3};{\mathbb{R}}^{3}))\,,
and

\ \mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\sigma}({\mathbb{T}}^{3},{\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
, with
\ \mathfrak{s}<\sigma<(5/2)^{+}
, such that

U^{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{S}(t/\varepsilon)\,\mathcal{U}\,+\,\mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon},\ \mbox{ with}\ \ \mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt}0\ \ \mbox{ \rm in }\ L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\sigma}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\!-\!\mbox{\rm strong}\,.

(67)

2.

There exists a function
\uppi_{0}:=\Delta_{\perp}^{-1}\nabla_{\perp}\cdot\,{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}u_{0\perp}\in L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu};H^{1}({\mathbb{T}}_{\perp}^{2}))
, as well as a function
\uppi_{1}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-r}({\mathbb{T}}^{3};{\mathbb{R}}))
, with
r>\big(\tfrac{5}{2}\big)^{+}
, such that

{\phi^{\varepsilon}}/{\varepsilon}\xrightharpoonup{\hskip 8.19447pt}\delta_{0}(t)\otimes\uppi_{0}\,+\,\uppi_{1}\ \ \mbox{ \rm in }\ H^{-1}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-r}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\!-\!\mbox{\rm weak}\,.

(68)

3.

The limit point
(B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu},\varrho)
satisfies the relation
B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}+b\varrho=0
, for a.e.
(t,x)\in]0,+\infty[\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3}
.

Proof.
On the one hand, applying the projector
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}
to the perpendicular component of the second equation of (
3
) (the one for
\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
) to form an equation for
\Phi^{\varepsilon}:={\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})
and on the other hand combining the first equation of (
3
) (the one for
\rho^{\varepsilon}
) and the parallel component of the second equation of (
3
) (the one for
B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}
) to form an equation for
\phi^{\varepsilon}:=b^{\varepsilon}\varrho^{\varepsilon}+B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}
, we obtain the following equation for
U^{\varepsilon}:={}^{t}(\phi^{\varepsilon},{}^{t}\Phi^{\varepsilon})
,

\partial_{t}U^{\varepsilon}-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathcal{L}U^{\varepsilon}=F^{\varepsilon}\,,

(69)

where

F^{\varepsilon}:=\left(\begin{aligned} F_{1}^{\varepsilon}\\
F_{2}^{\varepsilon}\end{aligned}\right)=\left\{\begin{aligned} F_{1}^{\varepsilon}=&-b^{\varepsilon}\partial_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon})+(\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}})^{-1}\nabla_{\perp}\cdot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}(\varrho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})-B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{\varepsilon}\cdot v^{\varepsilon}\\
&-(v^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{\varepsilon})B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}+(B^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{\varepsilon})v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}+(\eta_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\Delta_{\perp}+\,\eta_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\Delta_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu})B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\,,\\
F_{2}^{\varepsilon}=&-{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}\nabla_{\varepsilon}\cdot(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\otimes v^{\varepsilon})-(\gamma-1)\nabla_{\perp}\Pi_{2}(\rho^{\varepsilon})-\tfrac{1}{2}\nabla_{\perp}(|B^{\varepsilon}|^{2})+\partial_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}B_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\\
&+{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}\nabla_{\varepsilon}\cdot(B_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\otimes B^{\varepsilon})+\mu_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{\perp}(\nabla_{\perp}\cdot v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})+\mu_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\Delta_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}+\lambda^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{\perp}(\nabla_{\varepsilon}\cdot v^{\varepsilon})\,.\end{aligned}\right.

(70)

We start with point
1
of Lemma
4
. We aim at showing (
67
) in two steps. The first step concerns the existence of a filtered profile
\,\mathcal{U}={}^{t}(\psi,{}^{t}\Psi)
, while the second one establishes its regularity in
L^{2}
.

Step 1
. Here, we show that the filtered solution
{}^{t}(\psi^{\varepsilon},{}^{t}\Psi^{\varepsilon})\equiv\,\mathcal{U}^{\varepsilon}:=\mathcal{S}(-t/\varepsilon)U^{\varepsilon}
is relatively compact in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\sigma}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
for
\sigma>\mathfrak{s}
. For this, we first show uniform bounds for
U^{\varepsilon}
in suitable functional spaces. Second, we use the fact that the group
\mathcal{S}
is an isometry in
H^{\alpha}
(
\alpha\in{\mathbb{R}}
) in order to obtain similar bounds on
(\,\mathcal{U}^{\varepsilon},\,\partial_{t}\,\mathcal{U}^{\varepsilon})
. Next, we invoke an Aubin–Lions theorem to obtain compactness of the sequence
\,\mathcal{U}^{\varepsilon}
and the existence of the filtered profile
\,\mathcal{U}
. Finally, using the isometry
\mathcal{S}
and the averaged profile
\,\mathcal{U}
, we construct an approximation to
U^{\varepsilon}
, with an error estimate which converges strongly as indicated in (
67
). In this way, we bypass the singularity in
1/\varepsilon
in (
69
).

Recall that
\kappa=\min\{2,\gamma\}
. Since
\varrho^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\kappa}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
and
B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
, we can assert that
\phi^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};(L^{\kappa}+L^{2})({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
. From Sobolev embeddings and a duality argument, we obtain
L^{3/2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})\hookrightarrow H^{-\upalpha}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
, with
\upalpha\geq 1/2
. Since
\kappa>3/2
, then
L^{\kappa}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})\hookrightarrow H^{-\upalpha}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
and
(L^{\kappa}+L^{2})({\mathbb{T}}^{3})\hookrightarrow H^{-\upalpha}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
. Since
\upalpha
can be chosen such that
\upalpha\leq\mathfrak{s}
, then
(L^{\kappa}+L^{2})({\mathbb{T}}^{3})\hookrightarrow H^{-\mathfrak{s}}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
and
\phi^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\mathfrak{s}}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\hookrightarrow L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\mathfrak{s}}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
. From the fifth statement of Lemma
3
,
\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\mathfrak{s}}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
. Since
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}
(resp.
\mathcal{S}
) is a continuous map (resp. an isometry) in
H^{\alpha}
, with
\alpha\in{\mathbb{R}}
, then
\,\mathcal{U}^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\mathfrak{s}}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
.

We are now going to obtain a bound for
\partial_{t}\,\mathcal{U}^{\varepsilon}
. Using (
69
), a straightforward computation shows that
\partial_{t}\,\mathcal{U}^{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{S}(-t/\varepsilon)F^{\varepsilon}
. Let us show that
F^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-r}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
for
r>5/2+\delta
, and any
\delta>0
. We will then obtain
\partial_{t}\,\mathcal{U}^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-r}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
, because
\mathcal{S}
is an unitary group in
H^{\alpha}
, with
\alpha\in{\mathbb{R}}
.

Let us start with
F_{1}^{\varepsilon}
. First, observe that

B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{\varepsilon}\cdot v^{\varepsilon}+(v^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{\varepsilon})B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}-(B^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{\varepsilon})v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}=[\nabla_{\varepsilon}\times(B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\times v^{\varepsilon})]_{{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};W^{-1,3/2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
by using Hölder inequality and the energy estimate. Obviously, from the energy estimate, the last two diffusive terms of
F_{1}^{\varepsilon}
belong to
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
. Using
U^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\mathfrak{s}}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
, the first term of
F_{1}^{\varepsilon}
is in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\mathfrak{s}-1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
. It remains to bound the second term of
F_{1}^{\varepsilon}
. Using Hölder inequality we obtain
\varrho^{\varepsilon}v^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\mathfrak{q}}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
with
1/\mathfrak{q}=1/\kappa+1/6
. Observe that
\mathfrak{q}\in(6/5,3/2]
since
\kappa\in(3/2,2]
. The Sobolev embedding
H^{\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})\hookrightarrow L^{\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
, with
1/\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}=1-1/\mathfrak{q}=(5\kappa-6)/(6\kappa)
and
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}\geq 3/\kappa-1\in[1/2,1)
, implies by duality that
L^{\mathfrak{q}}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})\hookrightarrow H^{-\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
. Since
3/\gamma-1\geq 3/\kappa-1
, we can choose
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}=\mathfrak{s}
. Therefore, the second term of
F_{1}^{\varepsilon}
is bounded in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\mathfrak{s}-1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
, and we obtain
F_{1}^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};(H^{-\mathfrak{s}-1}+W^{-1,3/2}+H^{-1})({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\hookrightarrow L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\mathfrak{s}-1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
, where the previous injection results from Sobolev embedding.

We continue with an estimate for
F_{2}^{\varepsilon}
. From the continuous embedding
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}(L^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\hookrightarrow W^{-\delta,1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
which works for all
\delta>0
, and the energy estimate, the first and fifth terms of
F_{2}^{\varepsilon}
are uniformly bounded in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};W^{-1-\delta,1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
. From the energy estimate, the second and third terms of
F_{2}^{\varepsilon}
are uniformly bounded in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};W^{-1,1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
. From the following continuous embedding,
\forall\alpha\geq 0
,
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}(H^{\alpha}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\hookrightarrow H^{\alpha}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
, and the energy estimate, the fourth term of
F_{2}^{\varepsilon}
is uniformly bounded in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
. Obviously, from the energy estimate, the last three diffusive terms of
F_{2}^{\varepsilon}
belong to
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
. Therefore, we obtain
F_{2}^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};(W^{-1-\delta,1}+W^{-1,1}+H^{-1}+L^{2})({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\hookrightarrow L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-r}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
, with
r>5/2+\delta
, by using Sobolev embeddings.

Now, using Lemma
13
of Appendix
A
, with
\mathfrak{B}_{0}=H^{-\mathfrak{s}}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
,
\mathfrak{B}=H^{-\sigma}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
,
\mathfrak{B}_{1}=H^{-r}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
,
\mathfrak{s}<\sigma<r
, and
p=q=2
, we obtain that
\,\mathcal{U}^{\varepsilon}
is compact in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\sigma}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
. We deduce that there exists
\,\mathcal{U}\equiv{}^{t}(\psi,{}^{t}\Psi)\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\sigma}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
such that
\,\mathcal{U}^{\varepsilon}
converges strongly to
\,\mathcal{U}
in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\sigma}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
. Since
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}\Psi^{\varepsilon}=0
(resp.
\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,\psi^{\varepsilon}=0
),
\forall\varepsilon\geq 0
, then
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}\Psi=0
(resp.
\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,\psi=0
). Since the group
\mathcal{S}
is an isometry in
H^{\alpha}
(
\alpha\in{\mathbb{R}}
), we finally obtain (
67
).

Step 2
. To show that
\mathcal{U}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3};{\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
, we use the auxiliary variable
\widetilde{U}^{\varepsilon}:={}^{t}(\phi^{\varepsilon},{}^{t}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})
. We first establish two points which allow us to deal with a truncated version of
\widetilde{U}^{\varepsilon}
instead of
\widetilde{U}^{\varepsilon}
itself.

1) From the first statement of Lemma
1
and estimates (
59
) (which imply, via the De la Vallée Poussin criterion
[
16
]
, that
\varrho^{\varepsilon}
is spatially uniformly integrable in
L^{3/2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
, uniformly in time on any compact time interval), we obtain
\ \|\varrho^{\varepsilon}-\varrho^{\varepsilon}\mathbbm{1}_{\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq R}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\kappa}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}\rightarrow 0
, as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, where
R=+\infty
and
\kappa=2
, if
\gamma\geq 2
; and where
R\in(3/2,+\infty)
with
R
fixed, and
\kappa=\gamma
, if
\gamma<2
. Moreover, since
B^{\varepsilon}
is
2
-uniformly integrable in space-time, we obtain, from the first statement of Lemma
1
,
\ \|B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}-B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\mathbbm{1}_{\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq R}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}\rightarrow 0
, as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, for any
R\in(1,+\infty]
. Indeed, the
2
-uniform integrability comes from the Gagliardo–Nirenberg interpolation inequality
\|B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{10/3}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})}\leq\|B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+},L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}^{2/5}\|\nabla B^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+},L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}^{3/5}<\infty
(from uniform bounds of Lemma
2
) and the De la Vallée Poussin criterion.

2) From the sixth statement of Lemma
3
, we obtain
\|\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}-v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{q}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}\rightarrow 0
, as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, for
1/q=1/\gamma+1/6
.

We now set
\widetilde{U}_{R}^{\varepsilon}:={}^{t}(\phi_{R}^{\varepsilon},{}^{t}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})
, where
\phi_{R}^{\varepsilon}:=(b^{\varepsilon}\varrho^{\varepsilon}+B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon})\mathbbm{1}_{\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq R}
. Then, from Step 1, and the points 1) to 2) of Step 2, we obtain
\mathcal{S}(-t/\varepsilon)\widetilde{U}_{R}^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow\,\mathcal{U}
in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\sigma}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
–strong. Indeed, we have

S(-t/\varepsilon)\widetilde{U}_{R}^{\varepsilon}=\,\mathcal{U}+S(-t/\varepsilon)\,{}^{t}\big([\phi_{R}^{\varepsilon}-\phi^{\varepsilon}],{}^{t}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}[(1-\rho^{\varepsilon})v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}]\big)+\mathcal{S}(-t/\varepsilon)\mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon}.

(71)

Since the group
\mathcal{S}
is an isometry in
H^{\alpha}
(
\alpha\in{\mathbb{R}}
), from the points 1) to 2) of Step 2, the second term of the right-hand side of (
71
) vanishes as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};(L^{\kappa}+L^{2})({\mathbb{T}}^{3};{\mathbb{R}})\times L^{q}({\mathbb{T}}^{3};{\mathbb{R}}^{2}))
–strong, and thus in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\sigma}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
–strong since
(L^{\kappa}+L^{2})({\mathbb{T}}^{3})\hookrightarrow H^{-\upalpha}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
with
\kappa>3/2
, and
L^{q}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})\hookrightarrow H^{-\upsigma}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
with
1/q=1/\gamma+1/6
, and where
(\upalpha,\upsigma)
can be chosen such that
1/2\leq\upalpha\leq\mathfrak{s}\leq\upsigma<\sigma
. From (
67
), the third term of the right-hand side of (
71
) vanishes as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\sigma}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
–strong.

Finally, the first assertions of Lemma
2
and Lemma
3
, and inequality (
59
) show that
\widetilde{U}_{R}^{\varepsilon}
is bounded in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3};{\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
, uniformly with respect to
\varepsilon
. Since the group
\mathcal{S}
is an isometry in
H^{\alpha}
(
\alpha\in{\mathbb{R}}
), we deduce that
\,\mathcal{U}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3};{\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
, which ends the proof of the first point of Lemma
4
.

We now turn to the proof of the point 2 of Lemma
4
. Using the equation (
69
) on the component
\Phi^{\varepsilon}
, we can see that
\phi^{\varepsilon}/\varepsilon
satisfies,
\forall\,\psi_{\perp}\in H_{c}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{r}({\mathbb{T}}^{3};{\mathbb{R}}^{2}))
, with
r>0
large enough (specified further),

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}dt\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,\frac{\phi^{\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon}\nabla_{\perp}\cdot\psi_{\perp}=-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}v_{0\perp}^{\varepsilon})\cdot\psi_{\perp}(0)-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}dt\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,(\rho^{\varepsilon}-1)v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\cdot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}\partial_{t}\psi_{\perp}\\
-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}dt\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\cdot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}\partial_{t}\psi_{\perp}-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}dt\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,F_{2}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\psi_{\perp}\ =\ T_{0}^{\varepsilon}+T_{1}^{\varepsilon}+T_{2}^{\varepsilon}+T_{3}^{\varepsilon}\,.

(72)

From properties of initial conditions (see Section
2.3
) we have
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}v_{0\perp}^{\varepsilon})\rightharpoonup{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}u_{0\perp}={\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{0\perp}
in
L^{2\gamma/(\gamma+1)}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
–weak, and
u_{0\perp}=v_{0\perp}\in L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
. Defining
\uppi_{0}:=\Delta_{\perp}^{-1}\nabla_{\perp}\cdot\,{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}u_{0\perp}\in L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu};H^{1}({\mathbb{T}}_{\perp}^{2}))
, we then have
\nabla_{\perp}\uppi_{0}={\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}u_{0\perp}\in L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
. From Hölder inequality we obtain
|T_{0}^{\varepsilon}|\leq\|\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}v_{0\perp}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2\gamma/(\gamma+1)}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})}\!\!

\|\psi_{\perp}(0)\|_{L^{2\gamma/(\gamma-1)}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})}
. This and continuous Sobolev embeddings, imply that there exists a constant
\mathcal{C}_{0}
(uniform in
\varepsilon
) such that
|T_{0}^{\varepsilon}|\leq\mathcal{C}_{0}\|\psi_{\perp}\|_{H_{c}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{r}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}
, with
r>(5/2)^{+}
. From Hölder inequality we obtain
|T_{1}^{\varepsilon}|\leq\|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\gamma}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}\|v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{6}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}\|\partial_{t}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}\psi_{\perp}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{q^{\prime}}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}
, with
1/q^{\prime}=1-1/\gamma-1/6
. Then first, from Lemmas
1
and
3
, we obtain
T_{1}^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow 0
, as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Second, using continuous Sobolev embeddings, there exists a constant
\mathcal{C}_{1}
(uniform in
\varepsilon
) such that
|T_{1}^{\varepsilon}|\leq\mathcal{C}_{1}\|\psi_{\perp}\|_{H_{c}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{r}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}
, with
r>(5/2)^{+}
. Using Lemma
3
(in particular
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}=0
), we obtain
T_{2}^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow 0
, as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. From Hölder inequality, we obtain
|T_{2}^{\varepsilon}|\leq\|v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}\|\partial_{t}\psi_{\perp}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}
, which implies, together with Lemma
3
and continuous Sobolev embeddings, that there exists a constant
\mathcal{C}_{2}
(uniform in
\varepsilon
) such that
|T_{2}^{\varepsilon}|\leq\mathcal{C}_{2}\|\psi_{\perp}\|_{H_{c}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{r}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}
, with
r>(5/2)^{+}
. From the Step 1 of this proof, we know that
F_{2}^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-r}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
, for
r>5/2+\delta
, and any
\delta>0
. Since
\nabla_{\perp}\times F_{2}^{\varepsilon}=0
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
, there exists
f_{2}^{\varepsilon}=\Delta_{\perp}^{-1}\nabla_{\perp}\cdot F_{2}^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-r}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
(uniformly in
\varepsilon
) such that
F_{2}^{\varepsilon}=\nabla_{\perp}f_{2}^{\varepsilon}
. Therefore, there exists a function
\uppi_{1}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-r}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
such that
f_{2}^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup\uppi_{1}
in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-r}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
–weak. Moreover, we deduce that there exists a constant
\mathcal{C}_{3}
(uniform in
\varepsilon
) such that
|T_{3}^{\varepsilon}|\leq\mathcal{C}_{3}\|\psi_{\perp}\|_{H_{c}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{r}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}
, with
r>(5/2)^{+}
. In summary, we have shown that there exists a constant
\mathcal{C}:=\sum_{i=0,\ldots,3}\mathcal{C}_{i}
, uniform in
\varepsilon
, such that for
r>(5/2)^{+}
,

\bigg|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}dt\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,\nabla_{\perp}\Big(\frac{\phi^{\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon}\Big)\cdot\psi_{\perp}\bigg|=\bigg|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}dt\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,\frac{\phi^{\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon}\nabla_{\perp}\cdot\psi_{\perp}\bigg|\leq\mathcal{C}\|\psi_{\perp}\|_{H_{c}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{r}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}\,,

which means that
\nabla_{\perp}(\phi^{\varepsilon}/\varepsilon)
and a fortiori
\phi^{\varepsilon}/\varepsilon
belong to
H^{-1}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-r}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
, uniformly in
\varepsilon
. Moreover, we have proved that

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}dt\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,\frac{\phi^{\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon}\nabla_{\perp}\cdot\psi_{\perp}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}dt\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,\uppi_{1}\nabla_{\perp}\cdot\psi_{\perp}\,+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,\uppi_{0}\nabla_{\perp}\cdot\psi_{\perp}(0)\,.

These two properties establish the point 2 of Lemma
4
. Using (
68
), and Lemmas
1
and
2
, we obtain that
\phi^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup\phi=b\varrho+B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}=0
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
, and
b\varrho+B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}=0
in
L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\kappa}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
, with
\kappa>3/2
, which justify the point 3 of Lemma
4
.

\square

We are now able to justify the passage to the limit in equation (
21
) for
\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
. Let us start with the initial condition term. From the discussion about the properties of sequences of initial conditions in Section
2.3
(in particular the uniform bound (
27
) and the resulting convergences), we can pass to the limit, in the distributional sense, in the initial condition term of (
21
) to obtain the limit point
u_{0\perp}=v_{0\perp}={\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}u_{0\perp}+{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}u_{0\perp}
. Using (
68
) in equation (
21
), we observe that the term
-\nabla_{\perp}\uppi_{0}=-{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}u_{0\perp}
(coming from the weak limit of
\phi^{\varepsilon}/\varepsilon
) cancels the irrotational par
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}u_{0\perp}
of the previous limit point
u_{0\perp}
, so that the limit initial condition is simply
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}u_{0\perp}={\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{0\perp}
. This is consistent with the fact that in the limit equation the test function
\psi_{\perp}
can be chosen divergence-free, i.e.,
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}\psi_{\perp}=\psi_{\perp}
. Moreover, according to (
6
), the two conditions
\nabla_{\perp}\cdot v_{0\perp}=0
(or
\nabla_{\perp}\cdot u_{0\perp}=0
, since
u_{0\perp}=v_{0\perp}
) and
B_{0{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}}+b\varrho_{0}=0
are related to a preparation of the initial data to avoid fast time oscillations. Since the limit initial condition
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}u_{0\perp}
comes naturally without any preparation, then in our framework, we can deal with general data satisfying
\nabla_{\perp}\cdot u_{0\perp}=\nabla_{\perp}\cdot v_{0\perp}\not\equiv 0
.

We next deal with the linear terms of (
21
). Using weak convergence of
v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
(resp.
B^{\varepsilon}
) yielded by the first statement of Lemma
3
(resp. Lemma
2
) we can pass to the limit, in the distributional sense, in all linear diffusive terms (resp. the linear advection term
B_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\psi_{\perp}
) of (
21
). Using weak convergence for
B^{\varepsilon}
and strong convergence for
B_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
, which are supplied by Lemma
2
, we can pass to the limit in the quadratic nonlinear term
B_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\otimes B^{\varepsilon}:D_{\varepsilon}\psi_{\perp}
to obtain the term
B_{\perp}\otimes B_{\perp}:D_{\perp}\psi_{\perp}
. Using the identity
{\mathbb{I}}_{\perp}={\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}+{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}
, for the term involving time derivative in (
21
), we obtain,
\forall\,\psi_{\perp}\in\mathscr{C}_{c}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3};{\mathbb{R}}^{2})
,

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{t}\psi_{\perp}=-T_{1}^{\varepsilon}-T_{2}^{\varepsilon}+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}dt\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{t}\psi_{\perp}\,,

(73)

where the terms
T_{1}^{\varepsilon}
and
T_{2}^{\varepsilon}
are the same as in (
72
). For the same reasons than the ones invoked in the proof of the point 2 of Lemma
4
,
T_{1}^{\varepsilon}
and
T_{2}^{\varepsilon}
vanish as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Therefore, using (
73
) and Lemma
2
(in particular
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}=v_{\perp}
), we obtain that
\partial_{t}(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})\rightharpoonup\partial_{t}v_{\perp}
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
. Now in equation (
21
), we simultaneously deal with the magnetic pressure term
|B^{\varepsilon}|^{2}/2
, the singular fluid pressure term
p^{\varepsilon}/\varepsilon^{2}
(with
p^{\varepsilon}=a(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma}
), and the singular magnetic term
B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}/\varepsilon
. Setting
\mathfrak{p}^{\varepsilon}=p^{\varepsilon}/\varepsilon^{2}+B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}/\varepsilon+|B^{\varepsilon}|^{2}/2
, this term can be rewritten as
\mathfrak{p}^{\varepsilon}=\phi^{\varepsilon}/\varepsilon+a(\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}})^{\gamma}/\varepsilon^{2}+\uppi_{2}^{\varepsilon}\,,
with
\,\uppi_{2}^{\varepsilon}=(\gamma-1)\Pi_{2}(\rho^{\varepsilon})+|B^{\varepsilon}|^{2}/2
. In
\mathfrak{p}^{\varepsilon}
, the constant term
a(\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}})^{\gamma}/\varepsilon^{2}
is irrelevant because it disappears by spatial integration in (
21
). From the point 2 of Lemma
4
, we obtain
\phi^{\varepsilon}/\varepsilon\rightharpoonup\uppi_{1}
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
. In fact, from (
68
) we have
\phi^{\varepsilon}/\varepsilon\rightharpoonup\delta_{0}(t)\otimes\uppi_{0}+\uppi_{1}
in
H^{-1}({\mathbb{R}}_{+},H^{-r}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
, but as already mentioned above, the term
\delta_{0}(t)\otimes\uppi_{0}
cancels the irrotational part
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}u_{0\perp}
of the limit term
u_{0\perp}
, so that the limit initial condition is
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}u_{0\perp}={\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{0\perp}
. From energy inequality (
30
)-(
33
) with the pressure term
\Pi_{2}
, we obtain
\uppi_{2}^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
, uniformly with respect to
\varepsilon
. Then, by weak compactness, there exists a function
\uppi_{2}\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
such that
\uppi_{2}^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup\uppi_{2}
in
L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
–weak
-*
. Therefore, we obtain
\mathfrak{p}^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup(\uppi_{1}+\uppi_{2})
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
.

Remark 1
.

Even if we have the strong convergence of
B_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})))
and a uniform bound in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
for
B^{\varepsilon}
, we do not have
|B^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\rightharpoonup|B|^{2}
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
. The reason of this lack of convergence comes from the fact that
\partial_{t}B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}
is not bounded, uniformly with respect to
\varepsilon
, in some suitable functional spaces. Indeed, equation (
24
) for
B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}
contains a singular term in
1/\varepsilon
, which prevents such a boundedness. In other words, this is fast time oscillations in the parallel direction that prevent time compactness and thus, such a convergence.

It remains to pass to the limit in the nonlinear term
\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\otimes v^{\varepsilon}
in (
21
). This is the purpose of the following lemma.

Lemma 5
.

There exists a distribution
\uppi_{3}\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
, such that

\nabla_{\varepsilon}\cdot(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\otimes v^{\varepsilon})\xrightharpoonup{\hskip 8.19447pt}\nabla_{\perp}\cdot(v_{\perp}\otimes v_{\perp})\ +\ \nabla_{\perp}\uppi_{3}\ \ \mbox{ in }\ \mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})\,.

Using Lemma
5
, we can complete the justification of the passage to the limit, in the distributional sense, in equations (
21
) to obtain the second equation of (
16
) in the sense of distributions.

Remark 2
.

In (
12
) the pressure term
\uppi
, which can be seen as a Lagrange multiplier ensuring the constraint
\nabla_{\perp}\cdot v_{\perp}=0
, comes from the following three contributions,
\uppi_{1}
,
\uppi_{2}
and
\uppi_{3}
. The pressure term
\uppi_{1}
comes from the singular fluid pressure term and the singular magnetic term. The pressure
\uppi_{2}
comes from the non-singular fluid and magnetic pressure terms. The pressure term
\uppi_{3}
comes from the Reynolds stress tensor.

Proof

of Lemma
5
.
Observe first the following decomposition,
\forall\,\psi_{\perp}\in\mathscr{C}_{c}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3};{\mathbb{R}}^{2})
,

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}dt\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\otimes v^{\varepsilon}:D_{\varepsilon}\psi_{\perp}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}dt\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\otimes v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}:D_{\perp}\psi_{\perp}

(74)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}dt\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\psi_{\perp}\ =\bar{\Gamma}_{1}^{\varepsilon}+\bar{\Gamma}_{2}^{\varepsilon}\,.

Using Hölder inequality, we obtain
|\bar{\Gamma}_{2}^{\varepsilon}|\leq\varepsilon\|\rho^{\varepsilon}|v^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}\|\partial_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\psi_{\perp}\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\infty}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}
. Therefore, exploiting the energy estimate, we have
\bar{\Gamma}_{2}^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow 0
, as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
.

To deal with the term
\bar{\Gamma}_{1}^{\varepsilon}
, we follow the spirit of the proof of the convergence result in the part III of
[
33
]
. For this we consider the following decomposition

\displaystyle\Gamma_{1}^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle:=\nabla_{\perp}\cdot(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\otimes v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})=\sum_{i=1}^{5}\Gamma_{1i}^{\varepsilon}=\nabla_{\perp}\cdot\big(\,\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\otimes{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\,+\,{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}

(75)

\displaystyle+\,({\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}[\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}]-\Upphi^{\varepsilon})\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\,+\,\Upphi^{\varepsilon}\otimes({\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}-\Upphi^{\varepsilon})+\Upphi^{\varepsilon}\otimes\Upphi^{\varepsilon}\,\bigr)\,,

where we set
\Upphi^{\varepsilon}:=\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\,\mathcal{U}
. We successively deal with the terms
\Gamma_{1i}^{\varepsilon}
, for
i\in\{1,\ldots,5\}
. We start with
\Gamma_{11}^{\varepsilon}
. Using the fourth and fifth assertions of Lemma
3
with
s=\upsigma\in[\mathfrak{s},1)
, we obtain
\Gamma_{11}^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup\nabla_{\perp}\cdot(v_{\perp}\otimes v_{\perp})
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
, which gives the first part of the limit in Lemma
5
. We continue with the term
\Gamma_{12}^{\varepsilon}
. Using the third and seventh assertions of Lemma
3
, since
1/q+1/6<1
, we obtain
\Gamma_{12}^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup 0
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
. For the term
\Gamma_{13}^{\varepsilon}
, we observe that
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}[\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}]-\Upphi^{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{R}_{2}^{\varepsilon}
, where the term
\mathcal{R}_{2}^{\varepsilon}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
is the second component of the error term
\mathcal{R}^{\varepsilon}={}^{t}(\mathcal{R}_{1}^{\varepsilon},{}^{t}\mathcal{R}_{2}^{\varepsilon})
involved in equation (
67
) of Lemma
4
. Using (
67
) with
\sigma=1
and the first or the third statement of Lemma
3
,
\Gamma_{13}^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup 0
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
. We pursue with the term
\Gamma_{14}^{\varepsilon}
. Let
\Upphi_{\eta}^{\varepsilon}
be a regularization of
\Upphi^{\varepsilon}
obtained by the following way. Using the fact that
\mathscr{C}_{c}^{\infty}
is dense in
L^{2}
, we define
\Upphi_{\eta}^{\varepsilon}:=\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\,\mathcal{U}_{\eta}
, where
\,\mathcal{U}_{\eta}\in\mathscr{C}_{c}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
is such that
\|\,\mathcal{U}_{\eta}-\,\mathcal{U}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}\leq\eta
, with
0\leq\eta\ll 1
. We consider the decomposition

\Upphi^{\varepsilon}\otimes({\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}-\Upphi^{\varepsilon})=(\Upphi_{\eta}^{\varepsilon}-\Upphi^{\varepsilon})\otimes({\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}-\Upphi^{\varepsilon})+\Upphi_{\eta}^{\varepsilon}\otimes({\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}-\Upphi^{\varepsilon})=:R_{1\eta}^{\varepsilon}+R_{2\eta}^{\varepsilon}\,.

(76)

For the term
R_{1\eta}^{\varepsilon}
, using the isometry property of
\mathcal{S}
, there exists a constant
C=C(\|\,\mathcal{U}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))})
such that
\|R_{1\eta}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}\leq C\eta
. For the term
R_{2\eta}^{\varepsilon}
, using the isometry of
\mathcal{S}
, we first observe that
\Upphi_{\eta}^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{m}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
, with
m\geq 0
, and for all
\eta>0
. Second, we claim that
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}-\Upphi^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow 0
in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\sigma}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
–strong, for
\mathfrak{s}<\sigma<(5/2)^{+}
. Indeed,
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}-\Upphi^{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{R}_{2}^{\varepsilon}+{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}((1-\rho^{\varepsilon})v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})
, where, using (
67
), we have
\mathcal{R}_{2}^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow 0
in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\sigma}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
–strong. From the sixth statement of Lemma
3
, the continuity of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}
in
L^{q}
with
1/q=1/\gamma+1/6
, and the embedding
L^{q}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})\hookrightarrow H^{-\upsigma}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
for
\upsigma\geq\mathfrak{s}
(cf. proof of Lemma
3
), we obtain
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}((1-\rho^{\varepsilon})v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})\rightarrow 0
in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\sigma}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
–strong, for all
\sigma\geq\upsigma
. Therefore, in the right-hand side of (
76
), taking first the limit
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
and then the limit
\eta\rightarrow 0
, we obtain
\Gamma_{14}^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup 0
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
. It remains to show that
\Gamma_{15}^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup\uppi_{3}\,
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
. For this, using Fourier series, we can compute explicitly the term
\Gamma_{15}^{\varepsilon}
. We set
\mathfrak{U}^{\varepsilon}\equiv{}^{t}(\upphi^{\varepsilon},{}^{t}\Upphi^{\varepsilon}):={}^{t}(\mathcal{S}_{1}(t/\varepsilon)\,\mathcal{U},{}^{t}\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\,\mathcal{U})=\mathcal{S}(t/\varepsilon)\,\mathcal{U}
, with
\,\mathcal{U}={}^{t}(\psi,{}^{t}\Psi)\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
, and such that
\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,\psi=0
, and
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}\Psi=0
. Since
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}\Psi=0
, then
\Psi=\nabla_{\perp}\uppsi
, with
\uppsi=\Delta_{\perp}^{-1}\nabla_{\perp}\cdot\Psi\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu};H^{1}({\mathbb{T}}_{\perp}^{2})))
. This regularity is deduced from the
L^{2}
regularity of
\,\mathcal{U}
. Similarly, since
\mathcal{S}
and
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}
commute, we obtain
\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,\upphi^{\varepsilon}=0
, and
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}\Upphi^{\varepsilon}=0
, so that
\Upphi^{\varepsilon}=\nabla_{\perp}\upvarphi^{\varepsilon}
, with
\upvarphi^{\varepsilon}=\Delta_{\perp}^{-1}\nabla_{\perp}\cdot\Upphi^{\varepsilon}
. We introduce the Fourier series

\psi=\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{3}}\psi_{k}(t)e^{{\rm i}k\cdot x}\,,\quad\Psi={\rm i}\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{3}}k_{\perp}\uppsi_{k}(t)e^{{\rm i}k\cdot x}\,,

with
\psi_{0}(t)=0
, and

\|\{\psi_{k}\}_{k}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};\ell^{2}({\mathbb{Z}}^{3}))}+\|\{\uppsi_{k}|k_{\perp}|\}_{k}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};\ell^{2}({\mathbb{Z}}^{3}))}=:\mathcal{N}_{0}<\infty\,,

(77)

where the last estimate comes from the
L^{2}
regularity of
\,\mathcal{U}
stated in Lemma
4
. We denote by
\mathfrak{U}_{k}^{\varepsilon}={}^{t}(\upphi_{k}^{\varepsilon},{}^{t}\Upphi_{k}^{\varepsilon}={\rm i}\,{}^{t}k_{\perp}\upvarphi_{k}^{\varepsilon})
the Fourier coefficients of
\mathfrak{U}^{\varepsilon}\equiv{}^{t}(\upphi^{\varepsilon},{}^{t}\Upphi^{\varepsilon}=\nabla_{\perp}\upvarphi^{\varepsilon})
. Inserting the Fourier series of
\mathfrak{U}^{\varepsilon}
in the linear equation
\partial_{t}\,\mathfrak{U}^{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{L}\,\mathfrak{U}^{\varepsilon}/\varepsilon
, we are led to solve linear second-order ODEs in time for the Fourier coefficients
\upphi_{k}^{\varepsilon}(t)
and
\upvarphi_{k}^{\varepsilon}(t)
, with the inital conditions
\mathfrak{U}_{k}^{\varepsilon}(0)=\,\mathcal{U}_{k}(t)
and
\partial_{t}\,\mathfrak{U}_{k}^{\varepsilon}(0)=\mathcal{L}_{k}\,\mathcal{U}_{k}(t)/\varepsilon
, where
\mathcal{L}_{k}={\rm i}\,{}^{t}(-c^{\varepsilon}k_{\perp}\cdot\,,\,{}^{t}k_{\perp})
. Solving these linear ODEs, we obtain for
\Upphi^{\varepsilon}
,

\Upphi^{\varepsilon}=\nabla_{\perp}\upvarphi^{\varepsilon}={\rm i}\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{3}}e^{{\rm i}k\cdot x}\,k_{\perp}\,\Big\{\uppsi_{k}(t)\cos\big(\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon}}|k_{\perp}|\tfrac{t}{\varepsilon}\big)-\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon}}|k_{\perp}|}\psi_{k}(t)\sin\big(\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon}}|k_{\perp}|\tfrac{t}{\varepsilon}\big)\Big\}\,.

We then obtain

\Upphi^{\varepsilon}\otimes\Upphi^{\varepsilon}=-\sum_{k,l\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{3}}e^{{\rm i}(k+l)\cdot x}\,\theta_{k}^{\varepsilon}(t)\theta_{l}^{\varepsilon}(t)(k_{\perp}\otimes l_{\perp})=-\big(S_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t,x)+S_{2}^{\varepsilon}(t,x)\big)\,,

(78)

with
\theta_{k}^{\varepsilon}(t)=\uppsi_{k}(t)\cos(\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon}}|k_{\perp}|t/\varepsilon)-\bigl({\psi_{k}(t)}/({\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon}}|k_{\perp}|})\bigr)\sin(\sqrt{c^{\varepsilon}}|k_{\perp}|t/\varepsilon)\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+})
,
\forall\,k\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{3}
, and where
S_{1}^{\varepsilon}
(resp.
S_{2}^{\varepsilon}
) is the sum of
(k,l)
on the subset
\Lambda_{1}=\{(k,l)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{Z}}^{3}\ ;\ |k_{\perp}|\neq|l_{\perp}|\}
(resp.
\Lambda_{2}=\{(k,l)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{Z}}^{3}\ ;\ |k_{\perp}|=|l_{\perp}|\}
). We next show that
S_{1}^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup 0
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
, and that
\nabla_{\perp}\cdot S_{2}^{\varepsilon}
is a perpendicular gradient. We first deal with
S_{1}^{\varepsilon}
. Let
\varphi(t,x)=\chi(t)\uplambda(x)\in\mathscr{C}_{c}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
. Then we obtain,

\langle S_{1}^{\varepsilon},\varphi\rangle\,=\,|{\mathbb{T}}^{3}|\!\!\!\sum_{(k,l)\in\Lambda_{1}}\!\!\!\!\uplambda_{k+l}\,(k_{\perp}\otimes\,l_{\perp})\!\!\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}\!\!\!\!dt\,\theta_{k}^{\varepsilon}(t)\theta_{l}^{\varepsilon}(t)\chi(t)\,=\!\!\!\!\sum_{(k,l)\in\Lambda_{1}}\!\!\!\!\mathcal{H}_{kl}^{\varepsilon}\,,

(79)

where
\uplambda_{k}
is the Fourier coefficients of
\uplambda
