. Using Cauchy–Scwharz inequality in time, we obtain
|\mathcal{H}_{kl}^{\varepsilon}|\leq|{\mathbb{T}}^{3}|\|\chi\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+})}d_{k}d_{l}|\uplambda_{k+l}|
, with
d_{k}:=(|k_{\perp}|\|\uppsi_{k}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+})}+\|\psi_{k}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+})})\max\{1,1/\sqrt{\underline{c}}\}\geq|k_{\perp}|\,\|\theta_{k}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+})}
. Using this estimate, bound (
77
), and Cauchy–Schwarz inequality for one of the infinite sums in (
79
), we obtain
|\langle S_{1}^{\varepsilon},\varphi\rangle|\leq 2\mathcal{N}_{0}^{2}|{\mathbb{T}}^{3}|\,\|\chi\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+})}\max\{1,1/\sqrt{\underline{c}}\}^{2}\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{3}}|\uplambda_{k}|
. This last sum converges because, from the regularity of
\uplambda
, the Fourier coefficients
\uplambda_{k}
decrease enough with respect to
k
. Then
S_{1}^{\varepsilon}
is summable in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
. Now, using the Riemann–Lebesgue theorem and the condition
|k_{\perp}|\neq|l_{\perp}|
for
(k,l)\in\Lambda_{1}
, recombining the oscillating products implying
\cos
and
\sin
, we easily show that
\theta_{k}^{\varepsilon}\theta_{l}^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup 0
in
L_{\rm loc}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}_{+})
–weak. Using this vanishing limit and the summability of
S_{1}^{\varepsilon}
, we obtain
S_{1}^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup 0
, in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
. We next deal with
S_{2}^{\varepsilon}
. With the same arguments as those used for
S_{1}^{\varepsilon}
,
S_{2}^{\varepsilon}
is summable in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
. It remains to show that
\nabla_{\perp}\cdot S_{2}^{\varepsilon}
is a perpendicular gradient. Symmetrizing the sum
S_{2}^{\varepsilon}
in
(k,l)
(such that the expression of the general term remains invariant by exchanging
k
and
l
), using the change of variable
l=n-k
in
S_{2}^{\varepsilon}
, and applying the divergence operator
\nabla_{\perp}\cdot\,
to
S_{2}^{\varepsilon}
, we obtain

\nabla_{\perp}\cdot S_{2}^{\varepsilon}=\frac{\rm i}{2}\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{3}}e^{{\rm i}n\cdot x}\!\!\sum_{|k_{\perp}|=|n_{\perp}-\,k_{\perp}|}\{(k_{\perp}\otimes[n_{\perp}-k_{\perp}])+([n_{\perp}-k_{\perp}]\otimes k_{\perp})\}\,n_{\perp}\theta_{n}^{\varepsilon}\theta_{n-k}^{\varepsilon}\,.

To simplify this expression, we first observe that
|k_{\perp}|=|n_{\perp}-k_{\perp}|
implies
|n_{\perp}|^{2}=2n_{\perp}\cdot k_{\perp}
. Using this identity, we obtain
(k_{\perp}\otimes[n_{\perp}-k_{\perp}])+([n_{\perp}-k_{\perp}]\otimes k_{\perp})=n_{\perp}(n_{\perp}\cdot k_{\perp})=n_{\perp}|n_{\perp}|^{2}/2
so that

\nabla_{\perp}\cdot S_{2}^{\varepsilon}=\nabla_{\perp}\Big(\tfrac{1}{4}\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{3}}|n_{\perp}|^{2}e^{{\rm i}n\cdot x}\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{3}}\theta_{n}^{\varepsilon}\theta_{n-k}^{\varepsilon}\Big)=:-\nabla_{\perp}\uppi_{3}^{\varepsilon}\xrightharpoonup{\hskip 8.19447pt}-\nabla_{\perp}\uppi_{3}\ \ \ \mbox{ in}\ \ \mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})\,.

This ends the proof of Lemma
5
.

\square

4.6.
Passage to the limit in the equation for
\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}^{\varepsilon}

Here, we justify the passage to the limit in equation (
22
) for
\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}
. Let us start with the initial condition term. From the discussion about the properties of sequences of initial conditions in Section
2.3
(in particular the uniform bound (
27
) and the resulting convergences), we can pass to the limit, in the distributional sense, in the initial condition term of (
22
) to obtain the limit initial condition
u_{0{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}}=v_{0{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}}
(since
u_{0}=v_{0}
). Next, using weak convergence of
v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}
yielded by the first statement of Lemma
3
, we can pass to the limit, in the distributional sense, in all linear diffusive terms of (
22
). Using the same arguments as those used to deal with (
73
) and show that
\partial_{t}(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})\rightharpoonup\partial_{t}v_{\perp}
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
, we obtain
\partial_{t}(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon})\rightharpoonup\partial_{t}v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
. Regarding the terms
\varepsilon\lambda^{\varepsilon}v^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{\varepsilon}\partial_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\psi_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}
and
\varepsilon(|B^{\varepsilon}|/2)\partial_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\psi_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}
, uniform bounds in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
for
v^{\varepsilon}
and
B^{\varepsilon}
, given by the energy estimate, show that the terms
\varepsilon\lambda^{\varepsilon}\partial_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\nabla_{\varepsilon}\cdot v^{\varepsilon}
and
\varepsilon\partial_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}(|B^{\varepsilon}|/2)
vanish in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
, as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. For the term
B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\otimes B^{\varepsilon}:D_{\varepsilon}\psi_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}
, we consider the following decomposition,
\forall\,\psi_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\in\mathscr{C}_{c}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3};{\mathbb{R}})
,

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}dt\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\otimes B^{\varepsilon}:D_{\varepsilon}\psi_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}dt\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}B_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{\perp}\psi_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\ +\ \varepsilon\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}dt\,|B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\partial_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\psi_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\,.

(80)

Using the uniform bound in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
for
B^{\varepsilon}
, given by the energy estimate, the second term of (
80
) vanishes as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Using weak convergence of
B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}
and strong convergence of
B_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
, given respectively by the first and third statements of Lemma
2
, we obtain
\nabla_{\perp}\cdot(B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}B_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})\rightharpoonup\nabla_{\perp}\cdot(B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}B_{\perp})
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
, which ends the treatment of the first term of (
80
). Therefore, we obtain
\nabla_{\varepsilon}\cdot(B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\otimes B^{\varepsilon})\rightharpoonup\nabla_{\perp}\cdot(B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}B_{\perp})
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
. For the singular fluid pressure term
p^{\varepsilon}/\varepsilon
, we rewrite this term as
p^{\varepsilon}/\varepsilon=\varepsilon(\gamma-1)\Pi_{2}(\rho^{\varepsilon})+b^{\varepsilon}\varrho^{\varepsilon}+a(\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}})^{\gamma}/\varepsilon
. The constant term
a(\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}})^{\gamma}/\varepsilon
is irrelevant because it disappears by spatial integration in (
22
). From energy inequality (
30
)-(
33
) with the pressure term
\Pi_{2}
, we obtain
\varepsilon(\gamma-1)\Pi_{2}(\rho^{\varepsilon})\rightarrow 0
in
L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
–strong. From the second statement of Lemma
1
, we obtain
b^{\varepsilon}\varrho^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup b\varrho
in
L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\kappa}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
–weak
-*
. Therefore, we obtain
\partial_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}(p^{\varepsilon}/\varepsilon)\rightharpoonup b\partial_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\varrho=-\partial_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
, where for the last equality we have used the point 3 of Lemma
4
. It remains to deal with the term
\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\otimes v^{\varepsilon}:D_{\varepsilon}\psi_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}
, for which we consider the following decomposition,
\forall\,\psi_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\in\mathscr{C}_{c}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3};{\mathbb{R}})
,

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}dt\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\otimes v^{\varepsilon}:D_{\varepsilon}\psi_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}dt\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{\perp}\psi_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\ +\ \varepsilon\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}dt\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}dx\,\rho^{\varepsilon}|v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\partial_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\psi_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\,.

(81)

Since from the energy estimate,
\|\rho^{\varepsilon}|v^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}
is bounded uniformly with respect to
\varepsilon
, the second term of (
81
) vanishes as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Finally, it remains to deal with the first term of (
81
). This term is the most delicate, because we have only weak compactness for
\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}
and
v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
. Indeed, even if
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
converge strongly,
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
converge weakly (to zero, see Lemma
3
). Therefore, to pass to the limit in this term, we will use Lemma
14
of Appendix
A
, for which we show below that its hypotheses, splitted in three points, are satisfied. 1) From the fifth statement of Lemma
3
, we obtain
\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}
in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{6\gamma/(6+\gamma)}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
–weak. 2) The uniform bound
v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{6}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
and Lemma 4.3 in
[
7
]
imply
\|v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}(t,\cdot+h)-v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}(t,\cdot)\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{6}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}\rightarrow 0
, as
|h|\rightarrow 0
, uniformly with respect to
\varepsilon
. 3) From equation (
22
) for
\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}
, we obtain in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
,

\partial_{t}(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon})=-\nabla_{\varepsilon}\cdot(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\otimes v^{\varepsilon})+\partial_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\big(\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon}p^{\varepsilon}+\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}|B^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\big)+\nabla_{\varepsilon}\cdot(B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\otimes B^{\varepsilon})+\mu_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\Delta_{\perp}v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}+\mu_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\Delta_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}+\varepsilon\lambda^{\varepsilon}\partial_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\nabla_{\varepsilon}\cdot v^{\varepsilon}\,.

(82)

Using the energy estimate and the preceding bound for the pressure term
p^{\varepsilon}/\varepsilon
(already used in this section), we obtain
\partial_{t}(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon})\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};(W^{-1,1}+W^{-1,\kappa})({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))+L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\hookrightarrow L_{\rm loc}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};W^{-1,1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
. Gathering points 1) to 3), we can apply Lemma
14
of Appendix
A
with
g^{\varepsilon}=\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}
,
h^{\varepsilon}=v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
,
p_{1}=q_{1}=2
(
1/p_{1}+1/q_{1}=1
),
p_{2}=6\gamma/(6+\gamma)
, and
q_{2}=6
(
1/p_{2}+1/q_{2}=1/\gamma+1/3<1
, for
\gamma>3/2
), to obtain
\nabla_{\perp}\cdot(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})\rightharpoonup\nabla_{\perp}\cdot(v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}v_{\perp})
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
. In conclusion, we have shown that the weak formulation (
22
) converges to the second equation of (
16
) in the sense of distributions.

4.7.
Passage to the limit in a combination of the equations for
\varrho^{\varepsilon}
and
B_{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\parallel$}}^{\varepsilon}

The passage to the limit in the equation of
B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}
is more delicate, because, unlike what is done to treat the asymptotic limit in the perpendicular direction, here, we can not use the unitary group method to deal with the singular term in
1/\varepsilon
in equation (
24
) for
B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}
. From the study of the asymptotic limit in the perpendicular direction, more precisely the point 3 of the Lemma
4
, we observe a relation between
B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}
and
\varrho
, namely,
B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}+b\varrho=B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}+\mathbbmssit{p}=0
, where we define
\mathbbmssit{p}=a\gamma\varrho
. From this relation, the idea is to cancel the
1/\varepsilon
-singularity in equation (
24
) for
B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}
with the
1/\varepsilon
-singularity coming from the equation of
\varrho^{\varepsilon}
, this latter equation being obtained from equation (
20
) for
\rho^{\varepsilon}
. Indeed, from equation (
20
), we construct the following equation for
\varrho^{\varepsilon}/\overline{\varrho^{\varepsilon}}=(\rho^{\varepsilon}/\overline{\varrho^{\varepsilon}}-1)/\varepsilon
,
\forall\varphi\in\mathscr{C}_{c}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3};{\mathbb{R}})
,

\int_{\Omega}dx\,\frac{\varrho_{0}^{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}}}\varphi(0)+\int_{0}^{\infty}dt\int_{\Omega}dx\,\bigg(\frac{\varrho^{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\big(\partial_{t}+v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{\perp}\big)\varphi+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{\perp}\varphi+\frac{\rho^{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\partial_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\varphi\,\bigg)=0\,.

(83)

Let us define the auxilliary component

\mathbbmsl{B}_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}:=\frac{1}{\mathbbl{c}}\ \Bigl(B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}-\frac{\varrho^{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\Bigr)\,,\qquad\mathbbmsl{B}_{0{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}}^{\varepsilon}:=\frac{1}{\mathbbl{c}}\ \Bigl(B_{0{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}}^{\varepsilon}-\frac{\varrho^{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\Bigr)\,,\qquad\mathbbl{c}=1+\frac{1}{b}\,.

Taking
\varphi=\psi_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}
in (
83
), where
\psi_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}
is the same test function as the one used in equation (
24
), and substracting to (
24
), we obtain

\mathbbl{c}\int_{\Omega}dx\,\mathbbmsl{B}_{0{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}}^{\varepsilon}\,\psi_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}(0)+\mathbbl{c}\int_{0}^{\infty}dt\int_{\Omega}dx\,\big(\mathbbmsl{B}_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\,(\partial_{t}+v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{\perp})\psi_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\\
-v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\,B_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{\perp}\psi_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}-\frac{\rho^{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\,v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\,\partial_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\psi_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}+\eta_{\perp}B_{{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}}^{\varepsilon}\,\Delta_{\perp}\psi_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}+\eta_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}B_{{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}}^{\varepsilon}\,\Delta_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\psi_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\big)=0\,.

(84)

In the sense of distributions, this reveals slow dynamics on
\mathbbmsl{B}_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}
. Since
B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}+b\varrho=0
, the weak limit of
\mathbbmsl{B}_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}
is
\mathbbmsl{B}_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}=(B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}-\varrho)/\mathbbl{c}=B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}
. This means that, after a boundary layer near
t=0
, the asymptotic behavior of
\mathbbmsl{B}_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}
is similar to the one of
B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}
. And, because
B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}+b\varrho=0
, the time evolution of
B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}
provides information on the first-order pressure
\mathbbmssit{p}=b\varrho
or on the first-order density
\varrho
. Now, to exhibit the equation satisfied by
B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}
, we pass to the limit in (
84
). Let us start with the initial condition term. From the assumptions and the discussion about the properties of sequences of initial conditions in Section
2.3
, we have
\varrho_{0}^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup\varrho_{0}
in
L^{\kappa}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
–weak with
\kappa=\min\{2,\gamma\}
,
\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\rightarrow 1
and
B_{0{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}}^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup B_{0{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}}
in
L^{2}({\mathbb{T}}^{3})
–weak. It follows that
\mathbbmsl{B}_{0{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}}^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup\mathbbmsl{B}_{0{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}}:=(B_{0{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}}-\varrho_{0})/\mathbbl{c}
. Observe that
\mathbbmsl{B}_{0{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}}=B_{0{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}}
if and only if
B_{0{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}}+b\varrho_{0}=0
. In view of (
6
), the two conditions
\nabla_{\perp}\cdot v_{0\perp}=0
and
B_{0{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}}+b\varrho_{0}=0
are related to a preparation of the initial data to avoid fast time oscillations. Still, in our framework, we can incorporate general data satisfying
\mathbbmsl{B}_{0{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}}\not\equiv B_{0{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}}
(and also
\nabla_{\perp}\cdot v_{0\perp}\not\equiv 0
, see Section
4.5
).

It is obvious that
\partial_{t}\mathbbmsl{B}_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup\partial_{t}\mathbbmsl{B}_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}=\partial_{t}B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
. We next deal with the linear terms of (
84
). Using weak convergence of
B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}
, yielded by the first statement of Lemma
2
, we can pass to the limit, in the distributional sense, in all linear diffusive terms of (
84
). Using the fifth statement of Lemma
3
, we obtain
\partial_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon})\rightharpoonup\partial_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
. Using weak convergence of
v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}
and the strong convergence of
B_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
, given by respectively the first statement of Lemma
3
and the third statement of Lemma
2
, we obtain
\nabla_{\perp}\cdot(v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}B_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})\rightharpoonup\nabla_{\perp}\cdot(v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}B_{\perp})
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
. Finally it remains to pass in the limit in the term
\nabla_{\perp}\cdot(\mathbbmsl{B}_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})
. On the one hand, we have weak compactness for
\mathbbmsl{B}_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}
, and on the other hand, we have only weak compactness for
v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
, because, despite the strong compactness of solenoidal part
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
, the irrotational part
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
converges only weakly (see Lemma
3
). Therefore, to pass to the limit in this term, we will use Lemma
14
of Appendix
A
, for which we show below that its hypotheses, splitted in three points, are satisfied. 1) We first recall that
\mathbbl{c}\mathbbmsl{B}_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}=B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}-(\varrho^{\varepsilon}/\overline{\rho^{\varepsilon}})
. Using the second statement of Lemma
1
together with the first statement of Lemma
2
, for
\kappa=\min\{2,\gamma\}
, we obtain that
\mathbbmsl{B}_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};(L^{\kappa}+L^{2})({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\hookrightarrow L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\kappa}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
. Therefore, by weak compactness, we obtain
\mathbbl{c}\mathbbmsl{B}_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup\mathbbl{c}\mathbbmsl{B}_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}=B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}-\varrho

in
L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\kappa}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
–weak
-*
. 2) The uniform bound
v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{6}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
and Lemma 4.3 in
[
7
]
imply
\|v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}(t,\cdot+h)-v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}(t,\cdot)\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{6}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))}\rightarrow 0
, as
|h|\rightarrow 0
, uniformly with respect to
\varepsilon
. 3) Using the uniform
L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\kappa}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
–bound for
\mathbbmsl{B}_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}
, the uniform
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{6}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
–bound for
v^{\varepsilon}
, the uniform
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{6}\cap H^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
–bound for
B_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
, and the uniform
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{q}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))
–bound for
\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}
, with
q=6\gamma/(6+\gamma)
, we obtain from equation (
84
) and Hölder inequality

\begin{array}[]{rl}\partial_{t}\mathbbmsl{B}_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};W^{-1,6\kappa/(6+\kappa)}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))&+\,L_{\rm loc}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};W^{-1,3}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\\
&+\,L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};(W^{-1,q}+H^{-1})({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\hookrightarrow L_{\rm loc}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};W^{-1,1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}))\,,\end{array}

where the last continuous injection comes from Sobolev embeddings. Gathering points 1) to 3), we can apply Lemma
14
of Appendix
A
with
g^{\varepsilon}=\mathbbmsl{B}_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}
,
h^{\varepsilon}=v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
,
p_{1}=\infty
,
q_{1}=2
(
1/p_{1}+1/q_{1}<1
),
p_{2}=\kappa
, and
q_{2}=6
(
1/p_{2}+1/q_{2}\leq 2/3+1/6<1
, for
\gamma\,,\kappa>3/2
), to obtain
\nabla_{\perp}\cdot(\mathbbmsl{B}_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})\rightharpoonup\nabla_{\perp}\cdot(\mathbbmsl{B}_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}v_{\perp})
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3})
. In conclusion, the weak formulation (
84
) converges to

\mathbbl{c}\int_{\Omega}dx\,\mathbbmsl{B}_{0{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}}\,\psi_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}(0)+\mathbbl{c}\int_{0}^{\infty}dt\int_{\Omega}dx\,\big(B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\,(\partial_{t}+v_{\perp}\cdot\nabla_{\perp})\psi_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\\
-v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\,B_{\perp}\cdot\nabla_{\perp}\psi_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}-v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\,\partial_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\psi_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}+\eta_{\perp}B_{{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}}\,\Delta_{\perp}\psi_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}+\eta_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}B_{{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}}\,\Delta_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\psi_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\big)=0\,.

Knowing (by passing to the weak limit) that
\nabla_{\perp}\cdot B_{\perp}=0
, we recover the first equation of (
16
) with the initial data prescribed in (
17
).

5.
Asymptotic analysis in the whole space

This section is devoted to the proof of Theorem
2
. As in the periodic case, we first obtain some weak and strong compactness properties for the same sequences. Since in the whole case the density is only locally integrable in space, some of these compactness results need different proofs.

5.1.
Compactness of
\rho^{\varepsilon}
and
\varrho^{\varepsilon}

Here, we aim at proving the following lemma.

Lemma 6
.

There exists a generic constant
C>0
, which may depend on
C_{0}
,
a
, and
\gamma
such that the sequences
{\rho^{\varepsilon}}
and
\varrho^{\varepsilon}:=(\rho^{\varepsilon}-1)/\varepsilon
satisfy the following properties.

\displaystyle\|\rho^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L_{\rm loc}^{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\leq C\,,\quad{\rm and}\quad(\rho^{\varepsilon}-1)\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L_{2}^{\gamma}\cap H^{-\upalpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))\,,\quad\forall\,\gamma>1\,,\quad\upalpha\geq 1/2\,,

\displaystyle\|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\leq C\varepsilon^{2/\gamma}\,,\quad{\rm and}\quad\|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\leq C\varepsilon\,,\quad\forall\,\gamma\geq 2\,,

\displaystyle\|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L_{\rm loc}^{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\leq C\varepsilon\,,\quad{\rm for}\ {\rm all}\ \ 1<\gamma<2\,,

\displaystyle\rho^{\varepsilon}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt}1\ \ \mbox{ \rm in }\ L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L_{2}^{\gamma}\cap L_{\rm loc}^{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))\!-\!\mbox{\rm strong}\,,\quad\forall\,\gamma>1\,,

\displaystyle\|\varrho^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L_{2}^{\kappa}\cap L_{\rm loc}^{\kappa}\cap H^{-\upalpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\leq C\,,\quad\kappa=\min\{2,\gamma\}\,,\quad\upalpha\geq 1/2

\displaystyle\varrho^{\varepsilon}\xrightharpoonup{\hskip 8.19447pt}\varrho\ \ \mbox{ \rm in }\ L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L_{\rm loc}^{\kappa}\cap H^{-\upalpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))\!-\!\mbox{\rm weak--}\!\ast\,,\quad\kappa=\min\{2,\gamma\}\,,\quad\upalpha\geq 1/2\,.

Proof.
We start with the first bound of the first assertion of Lemma
6
. We first claim that
\rho^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L_{\rm loc}^{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
if
\rho^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L_{\rm loc}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
. Indeed, using the convexity of the power function
{\mathbb{R}}^{+}\ni x\mapsto x^{\gamma}
(with
\gamma>1
), and energy inequality (
30
)-(
32
) with the pressure term
\Pi_{3}
, there exists a constant
c_{0}
, such that for any compact set
K\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
,
0\leq\int_{K}dx\,\{(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma}-\gamma\rho^{\varepsilon}+\gamma-1\}\leq c_{0}
. Then,
\int_{K}dx\,(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma}\leq c_{0}+(\gamma-1)|K|+\gamma\int_{K}dx\,\rho^{\varepsilon}<\infty
, if
\rho^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L_{\rm loc}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
. It remains to prove this last property. For this, we consider a test function
\varphi\in\mathscr{C}_{c}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
, such that
\varphi\geq 0
, and
\varphi\equiv 1
on a subset
K
of its support
S
. From the mass conservation law and the energy estimate, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{S}dx\,\rho^{\varepsilon}\varphi=\int_{S}dx\,\rho^{\varepsilon}v^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\varphi\leq\|2\nabla\sqrt{\varphi}\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\bigg(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}dx\,\rho^{\varepsilon}|v^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\bigg)\bigg(\int_{S}dx\,\rho^{\varepsilon}\varphi\bigg)\leq\mathfrak{c}_{0}\bigg(\int_{S}dx\,\rho^{\varepsilon}\varphi\bigg)\,,

where the constant
\mathfrak{c}_{0}
depends on
C_{0}
and
\varphi
. This inequality leads to
\int_{K}dx\,|\rho^{\varepsilon}|\leq\int_{S}dx\,\rho^{\varepsilon}\varphi\leq\exp(\mathfrak{c}_{0}t)\int_{S}dx\,\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}\varphi
, which shows that
\rho^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L_{\rm loc}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
. It can also be shown from the second and third statements of Lemma
6
. Now, from the uniform bound (
36
) and energy inequality (
30
)-(
32
) with the pressure term
\Pi_{3}
defined by (
38
), we obtain
\Pi_{3}(\rho^{\varepsilon})\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
uniformly with respect to
\varepsilon
. From this bound and Lemma
12
(with
f=\rho^{\varepsilon}
and
\bar{f}=1
), we obtain, for any
T\in(0,+\infty)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}dx\,\{|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1|\leq\delta\}}+|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1|^{\gamma}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1|>\delta\}}\}=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}dx\,\mathfrak{Z}_{2,\delta}^{\gamma,1}(\rho^{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt\leq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\frac{1}{\kappa_{1}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}dx\,\Uppi_{1,\gamma}(\rho^{\varepsilon})=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\frac{(\gamma-1)\varepsilon^{2}}{\kappa_{1}a}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}dx\,\Pi_{3}(\rho^{\varepsilon})\leq\frac{C_{0}(\gamma-1)\varepsilon^{2}}{\kappa_{1}a}\,.

(85)

This inequality implies the bound in
L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L_{2}^{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
in the second part of the first statement of Lemma
6
, and also the strong convergence of
\rho^{\varepsilon}
to
1
in
L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+},L_{2}^{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
in the fourth statement of Lemma
6
. To complete the proof of the second part of the first statement of Lemma
6
, we have to show that
L_{2}^{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})\hookrightarrow H^{-\upalpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
, with
\upalpha\geq 1/2
. This embedding is obvious for
\gamma=2
. For
\gamma\neq 2
, from the definition of the space
L_{2}^{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
, the density
\rho^{\varepsilon}(t)-1
can be splitted into parts
d_{1}(t)^{\varepsilon}:=(\rho^{\varepsilon}(t)-1)\mathbbm{1}_{\{|{\rho^{\varepsilon}(t)-1}|\leq\delta\}}\in L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
and
d_{2}^{\varepsilon}(t):=(\rho^{\varepsilon}(t)-1)\mathbbm{1}_{\{|{\rho^{\varepsilon}(t)-1}|>\delta\}}\in L^{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
. The part
d_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t)
is obviously in
H^{-\upalpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
with
\upalpha\geq 1/2
. For the part
d_{2}^{\varepsilon}(t)
we proceed as follows. By Sobolev embeddings we have
H^{\upalpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})\hookrightarrow L^{\gamma^{\prime}}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
with
1/\gamma+1/\gamma^{\prime}=1
, and
\upalpha>1/2\,
since
\gamma>3/2
. By duality we then obtain
d_{2}^{\varepsilon}(t)\in L^{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})\hookrightarrow H^{-\upalpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
. Therefore
(\rho^{\varepsilon}(t)-1)\in H^{-\upalpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
, with
\upalpha\geq 1/2
. We continue with the second assertion of Lemma
6
. Estimate (
5.1
) and the inequality
|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1|^{2}\geq|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1|^{\gamma}
, for
\gamma\geq 2
and
|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1|\leq\delta<1
, imply the first part of the second statement of Lemma
6
. The second part of this second statement appeals to Lemma
11
. Indeed, from this lemma, for
\gamma\geq 2
, we obtain
|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1|^{2}\leq\varepsilon^{2}(\gamma-1)/(\nu_{1}a)\Pi_{3}(\rho^{\varepsilon})
, which gives
\|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\leq\varepsilon\sqrt{C_{0}(\gamma-1)/(\nu_{1}a)}
. We continue with the third assertion of Lemma
6
. Using Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and estimate (
5.1
), we obtain, for any compact set
K\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
, and any
T\in(0,+\infty)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\rho^{\varepsilon}(t)-1\|_{L^{\gamma}(K)}^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq|K|^{1-\gamma/2}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\bigg(\int_{K}dx\,|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|{\rho^{\varepsilon}-1}|\leq\delta\}}\bigg)^{\gamma/2}

\displaystyle\quad+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{K}dx\,|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1|^{\gamma}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|{\rho^{\varepsilon}-1}|>\delta\}}

\displaystyle\leq C(|K|,C_{0},a,\gamma)(\varepsilon^{\gamma}+\varepsilon^{2})\,,

which justifies the third assertion of Lemma
6
. Then, the strong convergence of
\rho^{\varepsilon}
to
1
in
L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+},L_{\rm loc}^{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
in the fourth statement of Lemma
6
, is obtained from the first part of the second statement of Lemma
6
, and the third statement of Lemma
6
. We continue with the fifth statement of Lemma
6
. The uniform bound in
L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L_{\rm loc}^{\kappa}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
for
\varrho^{\varepsilon}
comes from the second part of the second statement of Lemma
6
, and the third statement of Lemma
6
. For the uniform bound of
\varrho^{\varepsilon}
in
L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L_{2}^{\kappa}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
, we distinguish two cases according to the value of
\gamma
. For
\gamma\geq 2
, since
\kappa=2
, the second part of the second statement of Lemma
6
implies the fifth one. For
1<\gamma<2
, estimate (
5.1
) leads to

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}dx\,\Big\{\Big|\frac{\rho^{\varepsilon}-1}{\varepsilon}\Big|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\frac{\rho^{\varepsilon}-1}{\varepsilon}|\leq\frac{\delta}{\varepsilon}\}}+\varepsilon^{\gamma-2}\Big|\frac{\rho^{\varepsilon}-1}{\varepsilon}\Big|^{\gamma}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\frac{\rho^{\varepsilon}-1}{\varepsilon}|>\frac{\delta}{\varepsilon}\}}\Big\}\leq\frac{C_{0}(\gamma-1)}{\kappa_{1}a}\,,

for any
T\in(0,+\infty)
. This last estimate and inequality
\varepsilon^{\gamma-2}>1
, imply the bound of
\varrho^{\varepsilon}
in
L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L_{2}^{\kappa}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
. To complete the fifth assertion of Lemma
6
, we observe, as above, that we have the embedding
L_{2}^{\kappa}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})\hookrightarrow H^{-\upalpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
, with
\upalpha\geq 1/2
and
\kappa=\min\{2,\gamma\}
. Finally, the sixth statement of Lemma
6
is obtained from the fifth assertion and weak compactness properties. This ends the proof of Lemma
6
.

\square

5.2.
Compactness of
B^{\varepsilon}

Here, we aim at proving the following lemma.

Lemma 7
.

The sequence
{B^{\varepsilon}}
satisfies the following properties.

\displaystyle B^{\varepsilon}\xrightharpoonup{\hskip 8.19447pt}B\ \ \mbox{ \rm in }\ L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{6}\cap H^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))\!-\!\mbox{\rm weak}\cap L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))\!-\!\mbox{\rm weak--}\ast\,,

\displaystyle\nabla_{\varepsilon}\cdot B^{\varepsilon}\xrightharpoonup{\hskip 8.19447pt}\nabla_{\perp}\cdot B_{\perp}=0\ \ \mbox{ \rm in }\ L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))\!-\!\mbox{\rm weak}\,,

\displaystyle B_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt}B_{\perp}\ \ \mbox{ \rm in }\ L_{\rm loc}^{r}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))\!-\!\mbox{\rm strong}\,,\quad 1\leq r<\infty\,.

Proof.
The proof of Lemma
7
is similar to the proof of Lemma
2
.

\square

5.3.
Compactness of
v^{\varepsilon}
and
\rho^{\varepsilon}v^{\varepsilon}

Here, we aim at proving the following Lemma.

Lemma 8
.

Assume
\gamma>3/2
. Let
\mathfrak{s}:=\max\{1/2,3/\gamma-1\}\in[1/2,1)
. The sequences
{v^{\varepsilon}}
and
\rho^{\varepsilon}v^{\varepsilon}
satisfy the following properties.

\displaystyle v^{\varepsilon}\xrightharpoonup{\hskip 8.19447pt}v\ \ \mbox{ \rm in }\ L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{6}\cap H^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))\!-\!\mbox{\rm weak}\,,

\displaystyle\nabla_{\varepsilon}\cdot v^{\varepsilon}\xrightharpoonup{\hskip 8.19447pt}\nabla_{\perp}\cdot v_{\perp}=0\ \ \mbox{ \rm in }\ L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))\!-\!\mbox{\rm weak}\,,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\xrightharpoonup{\hskip 8.19447pt}0\ \ \mbox{ \rm in }\ L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{6}\cap H^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))\!-\!\mbox{\rm weak}\,,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt}{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}=v_{\perp}\ \ \mbox{ \rm in }\ L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L_{\rm loc}^{p}\cap H_{\rm loc}^{s}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))\!-\!\mbox{\rm strong}\,,\quad 1\leq p<6\,,\quad 0\leq s<1\,,

\displaystyle\rho^{\varepsilon}v^{\varepsilon}\xrightharpoonup{\hskip 8.19447pt}v\ \ \mbox{ \rm in }\ L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L_{\rm loc}^{q}\cap H^{-\upsigma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))\!-\!\mbox{\rm weak}\,,\quad\forall\,\upsigma\geq\mathfrak{s}\,,\quad q={6\gamma}/({6+\gamma})\,,

\displaystyle\rho^{\varepsilon}v^{\varepsilon}-v^{\varepsilon}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt}0\ \ \mbox{ \rm in }\ L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L_{\rm loc}^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))\!-\!\mbox{\rm strong}\,,\quad q={6\gamma}/({6+\gamma})\,,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt}{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}=v_{\perp}\ \ \mbox{ \rm in }\ L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L_{\rm loc}^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))\!-\!\mbox{\rm strong}\,,\quad q={6\gamma}/({6+\gamma})\,.

Proof.
We start with the first statement of Lemma
8
. The proof of the first statement of Lemma
8
is similar to the one of the first statement of Lemma
3
except for the bound
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
. We consider the decomposition
v^{\varepsilon}=v_{1}^{\varepsilon}+v_{2}^{\varepsilon}
, where
v_{1}^{\varepsilon}:=v^{\varepsilon}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1|\leq\delta\}}
, and
v_{2}^{\varepsilon}:=v^{\varepsilon}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1|>\delta\}}
. For
v_{1}^{\varepsilon}
, since
|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1|\leq\delta
, we obtain
1-\delta\leq|\rho^{\varepsilon}|
and thus

\|v_{1}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\leq(1-\delta)^{-1/2}\|\rho^{\varepsilon}|v^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{+};L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}^{1/2}\leq\sqrt{C_{0}/(1-\delta)}\,,

(86)

where we have used energy inequality (
30
)-(
32
) with the pressure term
\Pi_{3}
defined by (
38
).
For
v_{2}^{\varepsilon}
, using in order, Hölder inequality (
1/\gamma+1/\gamma^{\prime}=1
), estimate (
5.1
), Gagliardo–Nirenberg interpolation inequality (
1/(2\gamma^{\prime})=\uptheta/2+(1-\uptheta)(1/2-1/3)
, i.e.,
\uptheta=1-3/(2\gamma)\in(0,1)
, since
3/2<\gamma<\infty
), and Young inequality (
ab\leq\uptheta a^{1/\uptheta}+(1-\uptheta)b^{1/(1-\uptheta)}
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}dx\,|v_{2}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}dx\,|v^{\varepsilon}|^{2}|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1|\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1|>\delta\}}

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{-1}\|(\rho^{\varepsilon}-1)\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1|>\delta\}}\|_{L^{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\|v^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2\gamma^{\prime}}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{-1}\bigg(\frac{C_{0}(\gamma-1)}{\kappa_{1}a}\bigg)^{1/\gamma}\varepsilon^{2/\gamma}\|v^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2\gamma^{\prime}}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{-1}\bigg(\frac{C_{0}(\gamma-1)}{\kappa_{1}a}\bigg)^{1/\gamma}\varepsilon^{2/\gamma}\|v^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})}^{2\uptheta}\|\nabla v^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})}^{2(1-\uptheta)}

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{-1}\bigg(\frac{C_{0}(\gamma-1)}{\kappa_{1}a}\bigg)^{1/\gamma}\varepsilon^{2/\gamma}\big(\uptheta\|v^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})}^{2}+(1-\uptheta)\|\nabla v^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})}^{2}\big)\,.

(87)

From energy inequality (
30
)-(
32
) with the pressure term
\Pi_{3}
, we obtain
\nabla v^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
,
uniformly with respect to
\varepsilon
. Using this last bound and (
86
), a local time integration of estimate (
87
) implies that there exist two constants
K_{0}
and
K_{1}
(depending on
C_{0}
,
\gamma
,
\kappa_{1}
,
a
and
\delta
) such that

\|v^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}^{2}\leq K_{0}+K_{1}\varepsilon^{2/\gamma}\|v^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}^{2}\,.

(88)

For
\varepsilon
small enough, this last inequality implies
v^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
and thus
v^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{6}\cap H^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
. We note that estimates (
87
)-(
88
) also imply
\varepsilon^{-\upeta}v_{2}^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
, uniformly with respect to
\varepsilon
, for
0\leq\upeta\leq 2/3
(since
\gamma>3/2
). We continue with the other statements of Lemma
8
. Using Lemma
6
and energy inequality (
30
)-(
32
) with the pressure term
\Pi_{3}
defined by (
38
), the proof of statements two to seven of Lemma
8
is similar to the proof of their counterparts of Lemma
3
for periodic domains.

\square

We continue with an auxiliary lemma, which will be useful to pass to the limit in the term
\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\otimes v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
. Contrary to the periodic case, we are not able to get the strong convergence (
67
) for
U^{\varepsilon}
. Indeed, we can still prove locally strong compactness in space-time for
\,\mathcal{U}^{\varepsilon}
(using an Aubin–Lions theorem and estimates (
92
)-(
93
) below), but returning to
U^{\varepsilon}
, via the group of isometry
\mathcal{S}
, such local strong compactness seems unaccessible since
\mathcal{S}
is non local. To recover some strong compactness for
U^{\varepsilon}
we consider a truncated version of
U^{\varepsilon}
and use the uniform integrability in space of such truncated sequences as well as the energy inequality and the strong convergence of
\rho^{\varepsilon}
.

Lemma 9
.

Assume
\gamma>3/2
. Let us define
U^{\varepsilon}:={}^{t}(\phi^{\varepsilon},{}^{t}\Phi^{\varepsilon})
, where
\phi^{\varepsilon}:=b\varrho^{\varepsilon}+B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon},\,
and
\ \Phi^{\varepsilon}:={\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}),\,
with
\ \varrho^{\varepsilon}:=(\rho^{\varepsilon}-1)/\varepsilon\,
. We also define
\,\mathcal{U}^{\varepsilon}:=\mathcal{S}(-t/\varepsilon)U^{\varepsilon}
, where the group of isometry
\{\mathcal{S}(\tau)\,;\tau\in{\mathbb{R}}\}
is the same as the one defined in Section
4.5
with now
\Omega={\mathbb{R}}^{3}
, except that we substitute
c
for
c^{\varepsilon}
. Finally, we recall that
\kappa=\min\{2,\gamma\}
, and we set
\upkappa=\max\{1/2,3/(2\gamma)\}\in[1/2,1)
and
\mathfrak{s}:=\max\{1/2,3/\gamma-1\}\in[1/2,1)
. Then,

1.

We have the following uniform (with respect to
\varepsilon
) bounds

\displaystyle\phi^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\upalpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))\,,\quad\upalpha\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\,,

(89)

\displaystyle\rho^{\varepsilon}v^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}\big({\mathbb{R}}_{+};\big(L^{2}+L^{2\gamma/(\gamma+1)}\big)\cap L_{\rm loc}^{2\gamma/(\gamma+1)}\cap H^{-\upbeta}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})\big)\,,\quad\upbeta\geq\tfrac{3}{2\gamma}\,.

(90)

\displaystyle\varrho^{\varepsilon}v^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}\big({\mathbb{R}}_{+};\big(L^{3/2}+L^{6\kappa/(6+\kappa)}\big)\cap L_{\rm loc}^{6\kappa/(6+\kappa)}\cap H^{-\varkappa}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})\big)\,,\quad\varkappa\geq\mathfrak{s}\,.

(91)

2.

There exists a constant
C
, independent of
\varepsilon
, such that

\displaystyle\|\,\mathcal{U}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\sigma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\leq C\,,\quad\sigma\geq\upkappa\,,

(92)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\,\mathcal{U}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-r}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\leq C\,,\quad r>\big(\tfrac{5}{2}\big)^{+}\,,

(93)

3.

There exists a function
\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}:={}^{t}(\widetilde{\psi}^{\varepsilon},{}^{t}\widetilde{\Psi}^{\varepsilon})\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3};{\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
,
a constant
C
, independent of
\varepsilon
, and a function
\upomega:{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\mapsto{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
, continuous in the neighborhood of zero, with
\,\upomega(\varepsilon)\rightarrow 0
, as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0_{+}
, such that, for
\sigma\geq\upkappa
,

\displaystyle\|\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\leq C\,,

(94)

\displaystyle\|\,\mathcal{U}^{\varepsilon}-\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\sigma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\lesssim\upomega(\varepsilon)\,,

(95)

\displaystyle\|U^{\varepsilon}-\mathcal{S}(t/\varepsilon)\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\sigma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\lesssim\upomega(\varepsilon)\,,

(96)

\displaystyle\|{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}-\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\sigma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\lesssim\upomega(\varepsilon)+\varepsilon\,.

(97)

Proof.
We start with the point 1 of Lemma
9
, beginning with (
89
). Using the fifth statement of Lemma
6
for an estimate of
\varrho^{\varepsilon}
, the first statement of Lemma
7
for an estimate of
B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}
, the embedding
(L^{\kappa}+L^{2})({\mathbb{R}}^{2})\hookrightarrow H^{-\upalpha}
, with
\alpha\geq 1/2
and
\kappa>3/2
, and the definition
\phi^{\varepsilon}:=b\varrho^{\varepsilon}+B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}
, we obtain (
89
). We continue with (
90
), by recasting
\rho^{\varepsilon}v^{\varepsilon}
as

\rho^{\varepsilon}v^{\varepsilon}=(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}v^{\varepsilon})\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1|\leq\delta\}}+(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}v^{\varepsilon})\frac{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}{\sqrt{|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1|}}\sqrt{|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1|}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1|>\delta\}}.

(98)

The first term of the right-hand side of (
98
) is the product of the function
\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}v^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
and the function
\rho^{\varepsilon}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1|\leq\delta\}}\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
, thus using Hölder inequality this product is in
L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
. The second term of the right-hand side of (
98
) is the triple product of the function
\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}v^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
, the function
\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}/\sqrt{|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1|}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1|>\delta\}}\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
, and the function
\sqrt{|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1|}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1|>\delta\}}\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
, thus, using Hölder inequality, this triple product is in
L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2\gamma/(\gamma+1)}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
. Using the Sobolev embeddings
\dot{H}^{\upbeta}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})\hookrightarrow\{L^{2\gamma/(\gamma-1)}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})\,,\ L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})\}
(with
\gamma>3/2
), for
\upbeta\geq 3/(2\gamma)
, by duality we obtain
\{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})\,,L^{2\gamma/(\gamma+1)}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})\}\hookrightarrow H^{-\upbeta}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
; hence (
90
). We continue with the proof of (
91
). We first consider the decomposition
\varrho^{\varepsilon}v^{\varepsilon}=\varrho^{\varepsilon}v_{1}^{\varepsilon}+\varrho^{\varepsilon}v_{2}^{\varepsilon}
, with
v_{1}^{\varepsilon}
and
v_{2}^{\varepsilon}
defined as in the proof of Lemma
8
. From
\varrho^{\varepsilon}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1|\leq\delta\}}\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
(fifth statement of Lemma
6
) and
v^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{6}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
, using Hölder inequality, we obtain
\varrho^{\varepsilon}v_{1}^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{3/2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
. From
\varrho^{\varepsilon}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1|>\delta\}}\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\kappa}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
(fifth statement of Lemma
6
) and
v^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{6}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
, using Hölder inequality, we obtain
\varrho^{\varepsilon}v_{2}^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\mathfrak{q}}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
, with
\mathfrak{q}=6\kappa/(6+\kappa)
. Moreover, using the first statement of Lemma
6
and Hölder inequality, we obtain
\varrho^{\varepsilon}v^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L_{\rm loc}^{\mathfrak{q}}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
. It remains to show the
H^{-\varkappa}
-bound in (
91
). Since
\varrho^{\varepsilon}v_{1}^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{3/2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
, using the Sobolev embedding
L^{3/2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})\hookrightarrow H^{-\upalpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
, with
\upalpha\geq 1/2
, we obtain
\varrho^{\varepsilon}v_{1}^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\upalpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
. Since
\varrho^{\varepsilon}v_{2}^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\mathfrak{q}}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
, using the Sobolev embedding
L^{\mathfrak{q}}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})\hookrightarrow H^{-\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}
with
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}\geq(3/\kappa)-1
while
(3/\kappa)-1\leq\mathfrak{s}
, we get
\varrho^{\varepsilon}v_{2}^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
. Combining this two last results, we obtain
\varrho^{\varepsilon}v^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\varkappa}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
, with
\varkappa\geq\mathfrak{s}
; hence (
91
).

We continue with the point 2 of Lemma
9
, starting with (
92
). Using (
89
)-(
90
) and since the group
\mathcal{S}
(resp. the operator
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}
) is an isometry (resp. continuous) in
H^{\alpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
with
\alpha\in{\mathbb{R}}
, we obtain (
92
). For (
93
), observe that
\partial_{t}\,\mathcal{U}^{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{S}(-t/\varepsilon)F^{\varepsilon}
, with
F^{\varepsilon}
defined by

F^{\varepsilon}:=\left(\begin{aligned} F_{1}^{\varepsilon}\\
F_{2}^{\varepsilon}\end{aligned}\right)=\left\{\begin{aligned} F_{1}^{\varepsilon}=&-b\partial_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon})+\nabla_{\perp}\cdot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}(\varrho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})-B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{\varepsilon}\cdot v^{\varepsilon}\\
&-(v^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{\varepsilon})B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}+(B^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla_{\varepsilon})v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}+(\eta_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\Delta_{\perp}+\,\eta_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\Delta_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu})B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\,,\\
F_{2}^{\varepsilon}=&-{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}\nabla_{\varepsilon}\cdot(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\otimes v^{\varepsilon})-(\gamma-1)\nabla_{\perp}\Pi_{3}(\rho^{\varepsilon})-\tfrac{1}{2}\nabla_{\perp}(|B^{\varepsilon}|^{2})+\partial_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}B_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\\
&+{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}\nabla_{\varepsilon}\cdot(B_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\otimes B^{\varepsilon})+\mu_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{\perp}(\nabla_{\perp}\cdot v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})+\mu_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\Delta_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}+\lambda^{\varepsilon}\nabla_{\perp}(\nabla_{\varepsilon}\cdot v^{\varepsilon})\,.\end{aligned}\right.

(99)

Using estimates of point 1 in Lemma
9
and following Step 1 in the proof Lemma
4
, we obtain from (
99
) and
\upkappa>\mathfrak{s}
(
\gamma>3/2
),
F_{1}^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};(H^{-\upkappa-1}+W^{-1,3/2}+H^{-1})({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))\hookrightarrow L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\upkappa-1}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
, and
F_{2}^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};(W^{-1-\delta,1}+W^{-1,1}+H^{-1}+L^{2})({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))\hookrightarrow L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-r}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
, with
r>5/2+\delta
, for any
\delta>0
. Therefore, using the isometry
\mathcal{S}
in
H^{\alpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
for any
\alpha\in{\mathbb{R}}
, we obtain (
93
).

We continue with the point 3 of Lemma
9
, starting with (
94
). For any
\delta>0
, we define
\widetilde{\psi}^{\varepsilon}:=\mathcal{S}_{1}(-t/\varepsilon)[\phi^{\varepsilon}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq 1+\delta\}}]
and
\widetilde{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}:=\mathcal{S}_{2}(-t/\varepsilon){\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho^{\varepsilon}\leq 1+\delta\}})
. Clearly, from the uniform bounds above and the isometry
\mathcal{S}
in
H^{\alpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
for any
\alpha\in{\mathbb{R}}
, we obtain
\widetilde{\psi}^{\varepsilon}
,
\widetilde{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
. We continue with the proof of (
95
). Since
\varrho^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L_{2}^{\kappa}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
and
B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
, using the De la Vallée Poussin criterion, we obtain that
\phi^{\varepsilon}
is spatially uniformly integrable in
L^{3/2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
, uniformly in time on any compact time interval. Then, from the fourth statement of Lemma
6
, we obtain
\|\phi^{\varepsilon}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho^{\varepsilon}>1+\delta\}}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{3/2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\rightarrow 0
, as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Therefore, using the isometry
\mathcal{S}
, we obtain the first part of (
95
), that is
\|\psi^{\varepsilon}-\widetilde{\psi}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\sigma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\rightarrow 0
, as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Since
\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho^{\varepsilon}>1+\delta\}}=\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho^{\varepsilon}>1+\delta\}}
, using
\rho^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow 1
in
L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L_{2}^{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))\!-\!\mbox{\rm strong}
(fourth statement of Lemma
6
), the uniform bound
\|\rho^{\varepsilon}|v^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\leq C<\infty
, and Hölder inequality, we obtain
\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\rho^{\varepsilon}>1+\delta\}}\rightarrow 0
in
L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2\gamma/(\gamma+1)}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
, as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Since the group
\mathcal{S}
(resp. the operator
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}
) is an isometry (resp. continuous) in
H^{\alpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
for any
\alpha\in{\mathbb{R}}
, using the embedding
L^{2\gamma/(\gamma+1)}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})\hookrightarrow H^{-\sigma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
(see above in the proof), we obtain the second part of (
95
), that is
\|\Psi^{\varepsilon}-\widetilde{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\sigma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\rightarrow 0
, as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Still using the isometry
\mathcal{S}
, we deduce estimate (
96
) from (
95
). It remains to prove (
97
). Using the continuity of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}
and (
96
), we obtain
\|{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}-\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\sigma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\leq\|(\rho^{\varepsilon}-1)v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\sigma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}+\upomega(\varepsilon)
. We split
(\rho^{\varepsilon}-1)v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
into the part
d_{1}^{\varepsilon}:=(\rho^{\varepsilon}-1)v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|{\rho^{\varepsilon}-1}|\leq\delta\}}
and the part
d_{2}^{\varepsilon}:=(\rho^{\varepsilon}-1)v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|{\rho^{\varepsilon}-1}|>\delta\}}
. Since
L^{3/2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})\hookrightarrow H^{-1/2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
, using Hölder inequality and estimate (
5.1
), we obtain
\|d_{1}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\sigma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\leq\|d_{1}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-1/2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\leq\|(\rho^{\varepsilon}-1)\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1|\leq\delta\}}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\|v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{6}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\lesssim\varepsilon
. For the part
d_{2}^{\varepsilon}
, we distinguish two cases according to the value of
\gamma
. For
\gamma\geq 2
, following the same proof as for
d_{1}^{\varepsilon}
, we obtain
\|d_{2}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\sigma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\lesssim\varepsilon
. For
3/2<\gamma<2
, we have
\upkappa=3/(2\gamma)>\mathfrak{s}=3/\gamma-1
(since
\gamma>3/2
). Then, using the Sobolev embeddings
L^{6\gamma/(6+\gamma)}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})\hookrightarrow H^{-\mathfrak{s}}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
, and estimate (
5.1
), we obtain
\|d_{2}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\sigma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\leq\|d_{2}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\upkappa}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\leq\|d_{2}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\mathfrak{s}}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\leq\|(\rho^{\varepsilon}-1)\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\rho^{\varepsilon}-1|>\delta\}}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\|v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{6}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\lesssim\varepsilon^{2/\gamma}\leq\varepsilon
. This ends the proof of Lemma
9
.

\square

5.4.
Passage to the limit in the compressible MHD equations

Here, we justify the passage to the limit in the weak formulation (
18
)-(
25
) of the MHD equations for the whole space. Using Lemmas
7
and
8
, the passage to the limit in equation (
23
) for
B_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
follows the same proof as the one of the periodic case described in Section
4.4
. Using Lemmas
6
,
7
and
8
, and following the sames lines as the ones of Section
4.5
for the periodic case, we can pass to the limit in almost all terms of equation (
21
) for
\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
. Indeed, the only difference with the proof of Section
4.5
is the justification of the limit
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
for the term
\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\otimes v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
that we detail below in Lemma
10
. In particular, the pressure term
\mathfrak{p}^{\varepsilon}=p^{\varepsilon}/\varepsilon^{2}+B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}/\varepsilon+|B^{\varepsilon}|^{2}/2
, rewritten as
\mathfrak{p}^{\varepsilon}=\phi^{\varepsilon}/\varepsilon+a/\varepsilon^{2}+\uppi_{2}^{\varepsilon}\,
, with
\,\uppi_{2}^{\varepsilon}=(\gamma-1)\Pi_{3}(\rho^{\varepsilon})+|B^{\varepsilon}|^{2}/2
, converges weakly to
\uppi_{1}+\uppi_{2}
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})
. More precisely,
\phi^{\varepsilon}/\varepsilon\rightharpoonup\delta_{0}(t)\otimes\uppi_{0}+\uppi_{1}
in
H^{-1}({\mathbb{R}}_{+},H^{-r}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
–weak, with
r>(5/2)^{+}
, and
\uppi_{2}^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup\uppi_{2}
in
L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
–weak
-*
, while the constant term
ba/\varepsilon^{2}
disappears by spatial integration in (
21
). As in the periodic case, the term
\delta_{0}(t)\otimes\uppi_{0}
cancels the irrotational part
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}u_{0\perp}
of the limit term
u_{0\perp}
, so that the limit initial condition is
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}u_{0\perp}={\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{0\perp}
. We also obtain
\phi^{\varepsilon}\rightharpoonup\phi=b\varrho+B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}=0
in
H^{-1}({\mathbb{R}}_{+},H^{-r}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
–weak, and the relation
b\varrho+B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}=0
holds for a.e.
(t,x)\in]0,+\infty[\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
, since
b\varrho+B_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};(L_{\rm loc}^{\kappa}+L^{2})({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
. Using Lemmas
6
,
7
and
8
, the passage to limit in equations (
22
) and (
84
), for respectively
\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu}^{\varepsilon}
and
\mathbbmsl{B}^{\varepsilon}
, is justified in a similar way as the one described in Sections
4.6
and
4.7
for the periodic case. Finally, to conclude the proof in the case of the whole space, we use the following lemma, which is the counterpart of Lemma
5
with a different proof since we do not have the strong convergence (
67
).

Lemma 10
.

There exists a distribution
\uppi_{3}\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})
, such that

\nabla_{\perp}\cdot(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\otimes v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})\xrightharpoonup{\hskip 8.19447pt}\nabla_{\perp}\cdot(v_{\perp}\otimes v_{\perp})\ +\ \nabla_{\perp}\uppi_{3}\ \ \mbox{ in }\ \mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})\,.

Proof.
First, we observe the following decomposition

\nabla_{\perp}\cdot(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\otimes v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})=\nabla_{\perp}\cdot(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\otimes{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})+\nabla_{\perp}\cdot({\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})+\nabla_{\perp}\cdot({\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})\,.

(100)

Using the fourth and fifth statements of Lemma
8
, for the first term of the right-hand side of (
100
), we obtain
\nabla_{\perp}\cdot(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\otimes v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})\rightharpoonup\nabla_{\perp}\cdot(v_{\perp}\otimes v_{\perp})
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})
. Using the third and seventh statements of Lemma
8
, for the second term of the right-hand side of (
100
), we obtain
\nabla_{\perp}\cdot({\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})\rightharpoonup 0
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})
. It remains to show that we have
\nabla_{\perp}\cdot({\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon})\rightharpoonup\nabla_{\perp}\pi_{3}
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})
, or equivalently, that, for any
\psi_{\perp}\in\mathscr{C}_{c}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})
such that
\nabla_{\perp}\cdot\psi_{\perp}=0
, we have

\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\Gamma^{\varepsilon}:=\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}dt\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}dx\,{}^{t}({\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}(\rho^{\varepsilon}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}))[D_{\perp}\psi_{\perp}]{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}=0\,.

(101)

Since
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
is bounded in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
uniformly with respect to
\varepsilon
, Using (
96
) with
\sigma\in[\upkappa,1]
, we obtain

|\Gamma^{\varepsilon}-\Gamma_{1}^{\varepsilon}|\lesssim\upomega(\varepsilon)\,,\quad\mbox{ with }\quad\Gamma_{1}^{\varepsilon}:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}dt\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}dx\,{}^{t}(\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon})[D_{\perp}\psi_{\perp}]{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\,.

(102)

Using the isometry
\mathcal{S}
in
H^{\alpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
for any
\alpha\in{\mathbb{R}}
, estimate (
112
), bound (
94
), and the fact that the group
\mathcal{S}
and the mollification operator
\mathcal{J}_{3,\updelta}
commute, we obtain, for any
\upmu\in[0,1]
and
\updelta\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\mathcal{J}_{3,\updelta}^{2}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}-\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\upmu}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathcal{J}_{3,\updelta}\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}-\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\upmu}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\quad+\quad\|\mathcal{J}_{3,\updelta}\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\mathcal{J}_{3,\updelta}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}-\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\mathcal{J}_{3,\updelta}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\upmu}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}

\displaystyle\hskip-142.26378pt\lesssim\updelta^{\upmu}(1+\|\chi_{\updelta}\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})})\|\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\lesssim\updelta^{\upmu}\,.

(103)

Since
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}
is bounded in
L_{\rm loc}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
uniformly with respect to
\varepsilon
, using (
103
), we obtain, for any
\upmu\in(0,1]
,

|\Gamma_{1}^{\varepsilon}-\Gamma_{2}^{\varepsilon}|\lesssim\updelta^{\upmu}\,,\quad\mbox{ with }\quad\Gamma_{2}^{\varepsilon}:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}dt\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}dx\,{}^{t}(\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\mathcal{J}_{3,\updelta}^{2}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon})[D_{\perp}\psi_{\perp}]{\mathbb{Q}}_{\perp}v_{\perp}^{\varepsilon}\,.

(104)

Since for any
\updelta>0
, and any
\upnu\geq 0
,
\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\mathcal{J}_{3,\updelta}^{2}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}\in L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{\upnu}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
, there exists a constant
C_{\updelta}
such that
\|\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\mathcal{J}_{3,\updelta}^{2}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{\sigma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\leq C_{\updelta}
, where
C_{\updelta}
explodes as
\updelta\rightarrow 0
. Then, using (
97
), we obtain

|\Gamma_{2}^{\varepsilon}-\Gamma_{3}^{\varepsilon}|\lesssim C_{\updelta}(\upomega(\varepsilon)+\varepsilon)\,,\quad\mbox{ with }\quad\Gamma_{3}^{\varepsilon}:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}dt\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}dx\,{}^{t}(\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\mathcal{J}_{3,\updelta}^{2}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon})[D_{\perp}\psi_{\perp}]\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}\,.

(105)

We now claim that

|\Gamma_{3}^{\varepsilon}-\Gamma_{4}^{\varepsilon}|\lesssim\updelta\,,\quad\mbox{ with }\quad\Gamma_{4}^{\varepsilon}:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}dt\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}dx\,{}^{t}(\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\mathcal{J}_{3,\updelta}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon})[D_{\perp}\psi_{\perp}]\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\mathcal{J}_{3,\updelta}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}\,.

(106)

Indeed, using in order
[\mathcal{S}\,,\,\mathcal{J}_{3,\updelta}]=0
(where
[\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,]
is the commutator),
L^{1}\ast L^{2}\subset L^{2}
, Cauchy–Scwharz inequality,
\|x\chi(x)\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\leq C<\infty
, estimate (
112
) (with
\sigma=s=0
), the isometry
\mathcal{S}
in
H^{\alpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
for any
\alpha\in{\mathbb{R}}
, and bound (
94
), we obtain

\displaystyle\Gamma_{3}^{\varepsilon}-\Gamma_{4}^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}dt\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}dx\,{}^{t}(\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\mathcal{J}_{3,\updelta}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon})[\mathcal{J}_{3,\updelta}\,,\,D_{\perp}\psi_{\perp}]\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}dt\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}dx\,{}^{t}(\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\mathcal{J}_{3,\updelta}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon})(x)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}dy\,\big(D_{\perp}\psi_{\perp}(y)-D_{\perp}\psi_{\perp}(x)\big)\chi_{\updelta}(x-y)\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}(y)

\displaystyle\leq\updelta\|D_{\perp}^{2}\psi_{\perp}\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\|\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\mathcal{J}_{3,\updelta}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\||\tfrac{x}{\updelta}\chi_{\updelta}|\ast(\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon})\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\updelta\|\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}^{2}\lesssim\updelta\|\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}^{2}\lesssim\updelta\,.

We now claim that there exists a constant
\widetilde{C}_{\updelta}
, which explodes as
\updelta\rightarrow 0
, such that for all
\upnu\geq 0
,

\Upgamma^{\varepsilon}:=\|\mathcal{J}_{3,\updelta}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}(t_{1})-\mathcal{J}_{3,\updelta}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}(t_{2})\|_{H^{\upnu}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\leq\widetilde{C}_{\updelta}\big(|t_{1}-t_{2}|+\upomega(\varepsilon)\big)

(107)

Indeed, using (
93
) and (
95
), we obtain

\displaystyle\Upgamma^{\varepsilon}
