\displaystyle\leq\|\chi_{\updelta}\|_{H^{\upnu+r}}\|{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}(t_{1})-{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}(t_{2})\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-r}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}+2\|\chi_{\updelta}\|_{H^{\upnu+\sigma}}\|\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}(t_{1})-{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}(t_{2})\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{-\sigma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{C}_{\updelta}\bigg(\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}d\tau\,\|\partial_{t}{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{H^{-r}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})}+\upomega(\varepsilon)\bigg)\leq\widetilde{C}_{\updelta}\big(|t_{1}-t_{2}|+\upomega(\varepsilon)\big)\,.

Time continuity estimate (
107
) allows us to replace the term
\mathcal{S}(t/\varepsilon)\mathcal{J}_{3,\updelta}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}
by its time regularization
\mathcal{J}_{1,\upeta}\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\mathcal{J}_{3,\updelta}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}
(with
\upeta>0
) since the error is controlled as

\|\mathcal{S}(t/\varepsilon)\mathcal{J}_{1,\upeta}\mathcal{J}_{3,\updelta}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}-\mathcal{S}(t/\varepsilon)\mathcal{J}_{3,\updelta}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{\upnu}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\leq\widetilde{C}_{\updelta}\big(\upeta+\upomega(\varepsilon)\big)\,,

(108)

for all
\upnu\geq 0
. Using (
108
), we then obtain

|\Gamma_{4}^{\varepsilon}-\Gamma_{5}^{\varepsilon}|\lesssim\widetilde{C}_{\updelta}\big(\upeta+\upomega(\varepsilon)\big)\,,\ \mbox{ with }\ \Gamma_{5}^{\varepsilon}:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{+}}dt\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}dx\,{}^{t}(\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\mathcal{J}_{1,\upeta}\mathcal{J}_{3,\updelta}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon})[D_{\perp}\psi_{\perp}]\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\mathcal{J}_{1,\upeta}\mathcal{J}_{3,\updelta}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}\,.

(109)

Therefore, by gathering estimates (
104
), (
105
), (
106
) and (
109
), and by first taking the limit
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, then the limit
\upeta\rightarrow 0
, and finally the limit
\updelta\rightarrow 0
, we observe that the proof of Lemma
10
is complete, if we prove
\Gamma_{5}^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow 0
, as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. This is the matter of the rest of the proof.

In other words, we just have to show
\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\Gamma_{5}^{\varepsilon}=0
, when
\mathcal{J}_{1,\upeta}\mathcal{J}_{3,\updelta}\,\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}
is replaced by a smooth version of
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\varepsilon}
, that we denote by
{\mathcal{V}}^{\varepsilon}
(to simplify the notation) with
{\mathcal{V}}^{\varepsilon}\in\mathscr{C}^{m}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{\upnu}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
for any
m\geq 0
and any
\upnu\geq 0
(not uniformly with respect to
\upeta
and
\updelta
). As in the proof of Lemma
5
, following the spirit of the proof of the convergence result in the part III of
[
33
]
, we introduce
\mathfrak{U}^{\varepsilon}\equiv{}^{t}(\upphi^{\varepsilon},{}^{t}\Upphi^{\varepsilon}):={}^{t}(\mathcal{S}_{1}(t/\varepsilon)\,\mathcal{V}^{\varepsilon},{}^{t}\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\,\mathcal{V}^{\varepsilon})=\mathcal{S}(t/\varepsilon)\,\mathcal{V}^{\varepsilon}
, with
\,\mathcal{V}^{\varepsilon}={}^{t}(\psi^{\varepsilon},{}^{t}\Psi^{\varepsilon})\in\mathscr{C}^{m}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{\upnu}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
, and we compute explicity
\nabla_{\perp}\cdot(\Upphi^{\varepsilon}\otimes\Upphi^{\varepsilon})
via Fourier transform. Since
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}\Psi^{\varepsilon}=0
, then
\Psi^{\varepsilon}=\nabla_{\perp}\uppsi^{\varepsilon}
, with
\uppsi^{\varepsilon}=\Delta_{\perp}^{-1}\nabla_{\perp}\cdot\Psi^{\varepsilon}
. This and commutation between
\mathcal{S}
and
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}
imply
{\mathbb{P}}_{\perp}\Upphi^{\varepsilon}=0
, so that
\Upphi^{\varepsilon}=\nabla_{\perp}\upvarphi^{\varepsilon}
, with
\upvarphi^{\varepsilon}=\Delta_{\perp}^{-1}\nabla_{\perp}\cdot\Upphi^{\varepsilon}
.
We introduce the Fourier decompositions

\psi^{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}d\xi\,e^{{\rm i}\xi\cdot x}\,\hat{\psi}^{\varepsilon}(t,\xi)\,,\quad\Psi^{\varepsilon}=\frac{{\rm i}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}d\xi\,e^{{\rm i}\xi\cdot x}\,\hat{\uppsi}^{\varepsilon}(t,\xi)\xi_{\perp}\,,

and denote by
\hat{\mathfrak{U}}^{\varepsilon}={}^{t}(\hat{\upphi}^{\varepsilon},{}^{t}\hat{\Upphi}^{\varepsilon}={\rm i}\,{}^{t}\xi_{\perp}\hat{\upvarphi}^{\varepsilon})
the Fourier transform of
{\mathfrak{U}}^{\varepsilon}\equiv{}^{t}(\upphi^{\varepsilon},{}^{t}\Upphi^{\varepsilon}=\nabla_{\perp}\upvarphi^{\varepsilon})
. Inserting the Fourier decomposition of
\mathfrak{U}^{\varepsilon}
in the linear equation
\partial_{t}\,\mathfrak{U}^{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{L}\,\mathfrak{U}^{\varepsilon}/\varepsilon
, we are led to solve linear second-order ODEs in time for the Fourier coefficients
\hat{\upphi}^{\varepsilon}(t)
and
\hat{\upvarphi}^{\varepsilon}(t)
, with the inital conditions
\hat{\mathfrak{U}}^{\varepsilon}(0)=\,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{\varepsilon}(t)
and
\partial_{t}\,\hat{\mathfrak{U}}^{\varepsilon}(0)=\hat{\mathcal{L}}\,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{\varepsilon}(t)/\varepsilon
, where
\hat{\mathcal{L}}={\rm i}\,{}^{t}(-c\,\xi_{\perp}\cdot\,,\,{}^{t}\xi_{\perp})
. Solving these linear ODEs, we obtain for
\Upphi^{\varepsilon}
,

\Upphi^{\varepsilon}=\nabla_{\perp}\upvarphi^{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}d\xi\,e^{{\rm i}\xi\cdot x}\,\frac{{\rm i}\xi_{\perp}}{|\xi_{\perp}|}\,\Big\{\hat{\mathfrak{m}}^{\varepsilon}(t,\xi)\cos\big(\sqrt{c}|\xi_{\perp}|\tfrac{t}{\varepsilon}\big)-\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{c}}\hat{\psi}(t,\xi)\sin\big(\sqrt{c}|\xi_{\perp}|\tfrac{t}{\varepsilon}\big)\Big\}\,,

where we have introduced
\hat{\mathfrak{m}}^{\varepsilon}=\hat{\uppsi}^{\varepsilon}|\xi_{\perp}|
(with

{\mathfrak{m}}^{\varepsilon}\in\mathscr{C}^{m}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{\upnu}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
,
\forall\,(m,\upnu)\geq 0
) to symmetrize the expressions.
We then obtain

\Upphi^{\varepsilon}\otimes\Upphi^{\varepsilon}=-\frac{1}{2(2\pi)^{6}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}d\xi\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}d\zeta\,e^{{\rm i}(\xi+\zeta)\cdot x}\,\theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\xi)\theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\zeta)(\xi_{\perp}\otimes\zeta_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}\otimes\xi_{\perp})\,,

(110)

with

\theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\xi)=\big(\mathfrak{m}^{\varepsilon}(t,\xi)/|\xi_{\perp}|\big)\cos(\sqrt{c}|\xi_{\perp}|t/\varepsilon)-\big({\psi(t,\xi)}/({\sqrt{c}|\xi_{\perp}|})\big)\sin(\sqrt{c}|\xi_{\perp}|t/\varepsilon)
. Then, we obtain

\displaystyle\nabla_{\perp}\cdot(\Upphi^{\varepsilon}\otimes\Upphi^{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle=-\frac{{\rm i}}{4(2\pi)^{6}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}d\xi\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}d\zeta\,e^{{\rm i}(\xi+\zeta)\cdot x}\,(\xi_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp})|\xi_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}\theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\xi)\theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\zeta)

\displaystyle\hskip-10.0pt+\frac{{\rm i}}{4(2\pi)^{6}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}d\xi\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}d\zeta\,e^{{\rm i}(\xi+\zeta)\cdot x}\,(\xi_{\perp}-\zeta_{\perp})(|\xi_{\perp}|^{2}-|\zeta_{\perp}|^{2})\theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\xi)\theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\zeta)\,.

(111)

The first term of the right-hand side of (
5.4
), is a gradient and thus its contribution in
\Gamma_{5}^{\varepsilon}
is null since
\nabla_{\perp}\cdot\psi_{\perp}=0
. In fact, following estimates below, we can show that this term converges in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})
to the pressure term
\nabla_{\perp}\uppi_{3}
, as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Then, it remains to show that the second term of the right-hand side of (
5.4
) vanishes in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})
, as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. In fact, because of the presence of the factor
1/|\xi_{\perp}|
in the definition of
\theta^{\varepsilon}
and since, contrary to the periodic case, we now have
\hat{\psi}(t,0)\neq 0
, we have to consider a truncated version of
\Upphi^{\varepsilon}\otimes\Upphi^{\varepsilon}
around low frequences (
\xi_{\perp}\simeq 0
). For this, for any
\delta\in(0,1)
, we consider
\psi_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}
,
\uppsi_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}
,
\mathfrak{m}_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}
, and
\Upphi_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}
defined by inverse Fourier transforms of
\hat{\psi}_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}:=\hat{\psi}^{\varepsilon}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\xi_{\perp}|\geq\delta\}}
,
\hat{\uppsi}_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}=\hat{\uppsi}^{\varepsilon}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\xi_{\perp}|\geq\delta\}}
,
\hat{\mathfrak{m}}_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}:=|\xi_{\perp}|\hat{\uppsi}_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}
and by
\Upphi_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}:=\mathcal{S}_{2}(t/\varepsilon)\,{}^{t}(\psi_{\delta}^{\varepsilon},\,{}^{t}\nabla_{\perp}\uppsi_{\delta}^{\varepsilon})
. Using Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, we then obtain the error term

\displaystyle\|\Upphi^{\varepsilon}\otimes\Upphi^{\varepsilon}-\Upphi_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}\otimes\Upphi_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}d\xi\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}d\zeta\,(|\hat{\mathfrak{m}}^{\varepsilon}(t,\xi)|+|\hat{\psi}^{\varepsilon}(t,\xi)|)\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\xi_{\perp}|<\delta\}}\,(|\hat{\mathfrak{m}}^{\varepsilon}(t,\zeta)|+|\hat{\psi}^{\varepsilon}(t,\zeta)|)\frac{(1+|\zeta|^{2})^{\upnu/2}}{(1+|\zeta|^{2})^{\upnu/2}}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\delta^{3/2}\big(\|{\mathfrak{m}}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}+\|\psi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\big)\bigg(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}d\zeta\,(|\hat{\mathfrak{m}}^{\varepsilon}(t,\zeta)|^{2}+|\hat{\psi}^{\varepsilon}(t,\zeta)|^{2})(1+|\zeta|^{2})^{\upnu}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\delta^{3/2}\big(\|{\mathfrak{m}}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{\upnu}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}^{2}+\|\psi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{\upnu}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}^{2}\big)\lesssim\delta^{3/2}\,,

for any
\upnu>3/2
. Therefore, we just have to show the claim for
\Upphi_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}
for each
\delta\in(0,1)
. This is equivalent to assuming that
\hat{\psi}^{\varepsilon}
and
\hat{\mathfrak{m}}^{\varepsilon}
vanish in
\xi_{\perp}
on a ball
\mathscr{B}_{\delta}
of radius
\delta
, independently of
(t,\xi_{\mkern 3.0mu\vphantom{\perp}\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 2.0mu\vrule depth=0.0pt\mkern 3.0mu},\varepsilon)
. With this assumption, we just need to estimate the second term of the right-hand side of (
5.4
) that we decompose as follows,

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}d\xi\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}d\zeta\,e^{{\rm i}(\xi+\zeta)\cdot x}\,(\xi_{\perp}-\zeta_{\perp})(|\xi_{\perp}|^{2}-|\zeta_{\perp}|^{2})\theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\xi)\theta^{\varepsilon}(t,\zeta)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}d\xi\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}d\zeta\,e^{{\rm i}(\xi+\zeta)\cdot x}\,(\xi_{\perp}-\zeta_{\perp})(|\xi_{\perp}|^{2}-|\zeta_{\perp}|^{2})\bigg\{

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\Big[\cos\big(\sqrt{c}(|\xi_{\perp}|+|\zeta_{\perp}|)\tfrac{t}{\varepsilon}\big)+\cos\big(\sqrt{c}(|\xi_{\perp}|-|\zeta_{\perp}|)\tfrac{t}{\varepsilon}\big)\Big]\frac{\hat{\mathfrak{m}}^{\varepsilon}(t,\xi)\hat{\mathfrak{m}}^{\varepsilon}(t,\zeta)}{|\xi_{\perp}||\zeta_{\perp}|}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\ \ \,-\Big[\sin\big(\sqrt{c}(|\xi_{\perp}|+|\zeta_{\perp}|)\tfrac{t}{\varepsilon}\big)+\sin\big(\sqrt{c}(|\xi_{\perp}|-|\zeta_{\perp}|)\tfrac{t}{\varepsilon}\big)\Big]\frac{\hat{\psi}^{\varepsilon}(t,\xi)\hat{\mathfrak{m}}^{\varepsilon}(t,\zeta)}{\sqrt{c}|\xi_{\perp}||\zeta_{\perp}|}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\ \ \,-\Big[\sin\big(\sqrt{c}(|\xi_{\perp}|+|\zeta_{\perp}|)\tfrac{t}{\varepsilon}\big)-\sin\big(\sqrt{c}(|\xi_{\perp}|-|\zeta_{\perp}|)\tfrac{t}{\varepsilon}\big)\Big]\frac{\hat{\mathfrak{m}}^{\varepsilon}(t,\xi)\hat{\psi}^{\varepsilon}(t,\zeta)}{\sqrt{c}|\xi_{\perp}||\zeta_{\perp}|}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\ \ \,-\Big[\cos\big(\sqrt{c}(|\xi_{\perp}|+|\zeta_{\perp}|)\tfrac{t}{\varepsilon}\big)-\cos\big(\sqrt{c}(|\xi_{\perp}|-|\zeta_{\perp}|)\tfrac{t}{\varepsilon}\big)\Big]\frac{\hat{\psi}^{\varepsilon}(t,\xi)\hat{\psi}^{\varepsilon}(t,\zeta)}{c|\xi_{\perp}||\zeta_{\perp}|}\bigg\}\,.

The eight terms in the right-hand side of the previous equation can be estimated in a similar way, hence we only treat one them, for instance,

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}d\xi\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}d\zeta\,e^{{\rm i}(\xi+\zeta)\cdot x}\,(\xi_{\perp}-\zeta_{\perp})(|\xi_{\perp}|^{2}-|\zeta_{\perp}|^{2})\sin\big(\sqrt{c}(|\xi_{\perp}|-|\zeta_{\perp}|)\tfrac{t}{\varepsilon}\big)\frac{\hat{\psi}^{\varepsilon}(t,\xi)\hat{\mathfrak{m}}^{\varepsilon}(t,\zeta)}{\sqrt{c}|\xi_{\perp}||\zeta_{\perp}|}\\
=\partial_{t}\mathcal{T}_{1}^{\varepsilon}+\mathcal{T}_{2}^{\varepsilon}\,,

where,

\mathcal{T}_{1}^{\varepsilon}=-\varepsilon\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}d\xi\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}d\zeta\,e^{{\rm i}(\xi+\zeta)\cdot x}\,(\xi_{\perp}-\zeta_{\perp})(|\xi_{\perp}|+|\zeta_{\perp}|)\cos\big(\sqrt{c}(|\xi_{\perp}|-|\zeta_{\perp}|)\tfrac{t}{\varepsilon}\big)\frac{\hat{\psi}^{\varepsilon}(t,\xi)\hat{\mathfrak{m}}^{\varepsilon}(t,\zeta)}{c|\xi_{\perp}||\zeta_{\perp}|}\,,

and

\mathcal{T}_{2}^{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}d\xi\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}d\zeta\,e^{{\rm i}(\xi+\zeta)\cdot x}\,(\xi_{\perp}-\zeta_{\perp})(|\xi_{\perp}|+|\zeta_{\perp}|)\cos\big(\sqrt{c}(|\xi_{\perp}|-|\zeta_{\perp}|)\tfrac{t}{\varepsilon}\big)\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\bigg(\frac{\hat{\psi}^{\varepsilon}(t,\xi)\hat{\mathfrak{m}}^{\varepsilon}(t,\zeta)}{c|\xi_{\perp}||\zeta_{\perp}|}\bigg)\,,

The proof of Lemma
10
will be complete if we prove that
\|\mathcal{T}_{1}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}
and
\|\mathcal{T}_{2}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}
vanish as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. Indeed, for any
\upnu>3/2
, using Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, we obtain, for any
T\in(0,+\infty)
,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{1}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}d\xi\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}d\zeta\,(|\xi_{\perp}|^{2}+|\zeta_{\perp}|^{2})\frac{|\hat{\psi}^{\varepsilon}(t,\xi)|}{|\xi_{\perp}|}\frac{|\hat{\mathfrak{m}}^{\varepsilon}(t,\zeta)|}{|\zeta_{\perp}|}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\bigg(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}d\xi\,\frac{1}{(1+|\xi_{\perp}|^{2})^{\upnu/2}}(1+|\xi_{\perp}|^{2})^{(\upnu+2)/2}|\hat{\psi}^{\varepsilon}(t,\xi)|\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\ \,\bigg(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}d\zeta\,\frac{1}{(1+|\zeta_{\perp}|^{2})^{\upnu/2}}(1+|\zeta_{\perp}|^{2})^{(\upnu+2)/2}|\hat{\mathfrak{m}}^{\varepsilon}(t,\zeta)|\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\|\psi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{\nu+2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\|{\mathfrak{m}}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{\nu+2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\lesssim\varepsilon\,,

and similarly,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{2}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\big(\|\partial_{t}\psi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{\nu+2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\|{\mathfrak{m}}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{\nu+2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}

\displaystyle\qquad+\|\psi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{\nu+2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\|\partial_{t}{\mathfrak{m}}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};H^{\nu+2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}\lesssim\varepsilon\,.

\square

Appendix A
Toolbox

This section collects lemmas frequently used throughout the text. We start with inequalities for the first-order Taylor expansion of the power function
x\mapsto x^{\gamma}
, with
\gamma>1
. These inequalities, mentioned in
[
33
]
, can be seen as “generalized” strong convexity properties of the power function on the non-negative half-line.

Lemma 11

(Convexity properties of the power function)
.

Let
\bar{x}>0
and
\gamma>1
be fixed positive real numbers. For all
R\in]\bar{x},+\infty[
, there exist positive constants
\nu_{i}
,
i=1,2,3
,
depending on
\gamma
,
R
and
\bar{x}
, such that

\begin{aligned} x^{\gamma}-\gamma x\bar{x}^{\gamma-1}&+(\gamma-1)\bar{x}^{\gamma}\\
&=x^{\gamma}-\bar{x}^{\gamma}-\gamma\bar{x}^{\gamma-1}(x-\bar{x})\geq\\
&\end{aligned}\left\{\begin{aligned} \nu_{1}\,|x-\bar{x}|^{2},&\ \ \ 0\leq x,\ \ \gamma\geq 2\,,\\
\nu_{2}\,|x-\bar{x}|^{2},&\ \ \ 0\leq x\leq R,\ \ 1<\gamma<2\,,\\
\nu_{3}\,|x-\bar{x}|^{\gamma},&\ \ \ R<x,\ \ 1<\gamma<2\,.\end{aligned}\right.

Proof.
Knowing that
\gamma>1
, the power function
{\mathbb{R}}^{+}\ni x\mapsto x^{\gamma}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{+}
is convex on
{\mathbb{R}}^{+}
, and strongly convex on any compact set of
{\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}
. The details are left to the reader.

\square

Recall that the Orlicz space
L_{2}^{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
is defined by (
34
), or see
Appendix A in
[
32
]
.

Lemma 12

(A criterion for belonging to the Orlicz spaces
L_{2}^{\gamma}(\Omega)
)
.

Let
\gamma>1
,
\bar{f}>0
and
\delta>0
be fixed positive real numbers. Let
f\in L_{\rm loc}^{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3};{\mathbb{R}}_{+})
be given. Define

\begin{array}[]{ll}\Uppi_{\bar{f},\gamma}:L_{\rm loc}^{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3};{\mathbb{R}}_{+})\rightarrow L_{\rm loc}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{3};{\mathbb{R}}_{+})\,,&\Uppi_{\bar{f},\gamma}(f):=f^{\gamma}-\gamma\bar{f}^{\gamma-1}f+(\gamma-1){\bar{f}}^{\gamma}\,,\\
\mathfrak{Z}_{2,\delta}^{\gamma,\bar{f}}:L_{\rm loc}^{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3};{\mathbb{R}}_{+})\rightarrow L_{\rm loc}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{3};{\mathbb{R}}_{+})\,,&\mathfrak{Z}_{2,\delta}^{\gamma,\bar{f}}(f):=|f-\bar{f}|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|f-\bar{f}|\leq\delta\}}+|f-\bar{f}|^{\gamma}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|f-\bar{f}|>\delta\}}\,.\end{array}

There exist two constants
\kappa_{1}
and
\kappa_{2}
, depending on
(\bar{f},\gamma,\delta)
, such that

\kappa_{1}\,\mathfrak{Z}_{2,\delta}^{\gamma,\bar{f}}(f)\leq\Uppi_{\bar{f},\gamma}(f)\leq\kappa_{2}\,\mathfrak{Z}_{2,\delta}^{\gamma,\bar{f}}(f)\,.

It follows that
\Uppi_{\bar{f},\gamma}(f)\in L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
if and only if
(f-\bar{f})\in L_{2}^{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
.

Proof.
The proof is long but straightforward. It mainly relies on Lemma
11
, a Taylor formula with integral remainder, and on the convexity (resp. strong convexity) of
x\mapsto x^{\gamma}
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{+}
(resp. on any compact set of
{\mathbb{R}}^{+}
). The details are left to the reader. Note that
this result is similar to the more general Lemma 5.3 in
[
32
]
to which we can also refer the reader for a proof.

\square

Lemma 13

(A space-time compactness lemma of Simon
[
43
]
)
.

Let
\mathfrak{B}_{0}\Subset\mathfrak{B}\subset\mathfrak{B}_{1}
be Banach spaces (the embedding
\mathfrak{B}_{0}\Subset\mathfrak{B}
is compact and the embedding
\mathfrak{B}\subset\mathfrak{B}_{1}
is continuous).
Let
I
be a compact interval. Fix
q
with
1<q\leq\infty
. Let
f_{\varepsilon}:I\rightarrow\mathfrak{B}
be a family of functions indexed by
\varepsilon
in a directed set
2
2

2

Since a directed set
[
6
,
28
]
is countable or uncountable, the one-parameter family of functions
\{f\}_{\varepsilon\in J}
, called sequences (respectively subsequences) by abuse of language, must be understood as generalized sequences (respectively subsequences) such as nets (respectively subnets) in the sense of Moore–Smith (see, e.g., Chapter 4, Sections 11 and 12 in
[
46
]
) or filters (respectively, finer filters) in the sense of Cartan (see, e.g., Chapter 1, Section 6 in
[
5
]
).

J
. Thus, for all
t\in I
, we have
f_{\varepsilon}(t)\in\mathfrak{B}
. We assume that
\{f_{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon\in J}
is bounded uniformly with respect to
\varepsilon
in
L^{q}(I;\mathfrak{B})\cap L^{1}(I;\mathfrak{B}_{0})

and that
\{\partial_{t}f_{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon\in J}
is bounded uniformly with respect to
\varepsilon
in
L^{1}(I;\mathfrak{B}_{1})
. Then
\{f_{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon\in J}
is relatively compact in

L^{p}(I;\mathfrak{B})
for all
p
with
1\leq p<q
.

We continue with the following space-time compactness lemma established and used in
[
32
]

for the proof of existence of global weak solutions to the compressible Navier–Stokes equations.

Lemma 14

(A space-time compactness lemma of P.-L. Lions
[
32
]
)
.

Let
\Omega
be
{\mathbb{T}}^{N}
or
{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
or an open set of
{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
. Let
J
be a directed set.
Let
\{g_{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon\in J}
, and
\{h_{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon\in J}
be sequences converging weakly to
g
and
h
, respectively in
L_{\rm loc}^{p_{1}}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{p_{2}}(\Omega))

and
L_{\rm loc}^{q_{1}}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{q_{2}}(\Omega))
, where
1\leq p_{1},\,p_{2}\leq\infty
and

\frac{1}{p_{1}}+\frac{1}{q_{1}}=1\,,\quad\frac{1}{p_{2}}+\frac{1}{q_{2}}=1\,.

Above, weak convergences are weak
-*
convergences whenever some of the exponents are infinite.
Assume, in addition, that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}g_{\varepsilon}\mbox{ is bounded in }L_{\rm loc}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};W^{-\alpha,1}(\Omega))\mbox{ for some }\alpha\geq 0\,,\mbox{ independent of }\varepsilon\,,

\displaystyle\|h_{\varepsilon}(t,\cdot)-h_{\varepsilon}(t,\cdot+\xi)\|_{L_{\rm loc}^{q_{1}}({\mathbb{R}}_{+};L^{q_{2}}(\Omega))}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt}0\ \mbox{as }\ |\xi|\rightarrow 0\,,\mbox{ uniformly in }\varepsilon\,.

Then, the sequence
\{g_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon\in J}
converges to
gh
in the sense of distributions in
{\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times\Omega
.

Proof.
This is Lemma 5.1 of
[
32
]
. The assumption in time is reminiscent to the Aubin–Lions
theorem. The assumption in space is reminiscent to the Kolmogorov–Riesz–Fréchet criterion
(e.g., see Theorem 4.26 in
[
7
]
) for compactness (”
L^{p}
-versions” of the Ascoli–Arzelà theorem).

\square

We end with results about mollifiers.

Lemma 15

(Mollification operators)
.

Let
\chi:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\mapsto{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
be a non-negative function belonging to
\mathscr{C}_{c}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d};{\mathbb{R}}_{+})
, and with total mass one. For any
\upeta\in(0,1)
,
define
\chi_{\upeta}(\cdot)=\upeta^{-d}\chi(\cdot/\upeta)
. The familly of non-negative functions of mass one
\{\chi_{\upeta}\}_{\upeta>0}
are called a family of mollifiers,
while the operator
\mathcal{J}_{d,\upeta}:\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})\mapsto\mathscr{C}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
,
defined as
\mathcal{J}_{d,\upeta}f=\chi_{\upeta}\ast f
, for any distribution
f
, is called a mollification operator. The mollification
operator
\mathcal{J}_{d,\upeta}
has the following approximation property. For all
f\in H^{s}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
, with
s\in{\mathbb{R}}
and any
\sigma\in{\mathbb{R}}
such that
0\leq s-\sigma\leq 1
, we have
the following approximation error estimate

\|\mathcal{J}_{d,\upeta}f-f\|_{H^{\sigma}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\lesssim\upeta^{s-\sigma}\|f\|_{H^{s}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\,.

(112)

Proof.
The property
\mathcal{J}_{d,\upeta}f=\chi_{\upeta}\ast f\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
for any
\upeta>0
and any
f\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
comes from the following convolution property
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})\ast\mathcal{D}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})\subset\mathscr{C}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
. For the proof of (
112
), observe

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{J}_{d,\upeta}f-f\|_{H^{\sigma}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}d\xi\,(1+|\xi|^{2})^{\sigma}\,|\widehat{\chi}(\upeta\xi)-1|^{2}\,|\hat{f}(\xi)|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\eta^{2(s-\sigma)}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}d\xi\,\frac{|\widehat{\chi}(\upeta\xi)-1|^{2}}{(\upeta\,|\xi|)^{2(s-\sigma)}}\,(1+|\xi|^{2})^{s}\,|\hat{f}(\xi)|^{2}\,.

Using the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem,
this last estimate leads to (
112
), since
\widehat{\chi}
is smooth at the origin with
\widehat{\chi}(0)=1
.

\square
